<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We construct a finitely generated torsion-free discrete group whose reduced group <i>C</i><sup>∗</sup>-algebra contains a projection other than zero and the identity. This disproves the Kadison–Kaplansky projection conjecture.

- **claim-scalar-counterexample** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There exist a finitely generated torsion-free discrete group $G_{\mathrm{proj}}$ and a projection $e\in C_r^*(G_{\mathrm{proj}})$ such that $$0<\tau_{G_{\mathrm{proj}}}(e)<\frac12.$$ In particular, $e$ is neither zero nor the identity.
  - Explanation: A finitely generated torsion-free group has a scalar reduced-algebra projection with strictly fractional canonical trace.
- **claim-reduced-subgroup-inclusion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-scalar-subgroup)): If $\Lambda$ is a subgroup of a discrete group $K$, the inclusion of group algebras extends to an isometric unital inclusion $C_r^*(\Lambda)\subseteq C_r^*(K)$, preserving the canonical traces and their finite matrix extensions.
  - Explanation: Subgroup inclusion preserves the reduced norm, the unit and the canonical matrix traces.
- **claim-free-product-scalar-compression** (construction, [source](#stmt-scalar-compression)): Let $\Lambda$ be a discrete group and $p\in M_N(C_r^*(\Lambda))$ a projection with $$0<a:=\tau_{\Lambda,N}(p)<\frac12.$$ Put $K=\Lambda*\mathbb F_N$, where $t_1,\ldots,t_N$ freely generate $\mathbb F_N$, and regard $p$ as a matrix over $C_r^*(K)$ by Lemma <a href="#scalar:subgroup" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="scalar:subgroup">2</a>. Set $$U=(\lambda_K(t_1),\ldots,\lambda_K(t_N)),\qquad A=pU^*Up.$$ Then $A$ is invertible in the unital corner $pM_N(C_r^*(K))p$, and $$e=(Up)A^{-1}(Up)^* \label{scalar:projection-formula}$$ is a projection in the scalar algebra $C_r^*(K)$ with $\tau_K(e)=a$. If $\Lambda$ is finitely generated and torsion-free, then so is $K$.
  - Explanation: A matrix projection of sufficiently small unnormalized trace becomes a scalar projection of the same trace after adjoining free generators.
- **claim-separated-matrix-target** (construction, [source](#stmt-setup-matrix-target)): There exist a finitely generated torsion-free discrete group $\Gamma_{\mathrm{proj}}$, a finite integer $N\ge1$, and a self-adjoint element $T\in M_N(\mathbb C[\Gamma_{\mathrm{proj}}])$, in its regular representation, with a nonempty separated spectral subset whose spectral projection $p_0$ satisfies $$p_0\in M_N(C_r^*(\Gamma_{\mathrm{proj}})),\qquad 0<\tau_{\Gamma_{\mathrm{proj}},N}(p_0)<\frac12.$$
  - Explanation: The intermediate target is a finite self-adjoint group-algebra matrix with a separated nonempty band and a small unnormalized projection trace.
- **claim-slot-functional-multiplicities** (construction, [source](#stmt-tow-coefficient-choice)): The injections and spills can be chosen so that the following holds for every family, separately on each side. Let $\mathcal I$ be the set of slots for its one adjacent pair, let $n_{\rm sl}=\|\mathcal I\|$, and let $$M_a:\mathbb F _2^s\longrightarrow\mathbb F _2^{s+\alpha} \quad\hbox{or}\quad M_c:\mathbb F _2^s\longrightarrow\mathbb F _2^{s+\beta}$$ be diagonal multiplication followed by its spill. For every nonzero linear functional $\zeta$ on the output space, every value of the restricted input functional $\zeta M_a$, or $\zeta M_c$, occurs on at most $0.55n_{\rm sl}$ slots after restriction to any nonempty initial segment of the $s$ input bits. On all $s$ input bits, the corresponding bound is $10^{-3}n_{\rm sl}$.
  - Explanation: Coefficient injections and spills simultaneously bound functional multiplicities on full blocks and nonempty prefixes.
- **claim-independent-cluster-matchings** (construction, [source](#stmt-tow-matching-clusters)): Fix an actual pair $(i,k)$ and every table at its lower marked positions. The current last-block tables can be sampled so that the remaining random choices in its $Y^0$-block are independent uniform perfect matchings between pairs of clusters, each cluster having $2^t$ slot incidences. There is one matching for each tuple consisting of the family, serial number, $x$, and all preceding $u$-blocks on both sides. Every edge in each cluster pair is a possible edge, and its matching is used only for that pair of clusters.
  - Explanation: Once lower tables are fixed, current tables provide independent uniform perfect matchings of slot-incidence clusters.
- **claim-conditional-fourier-variance** (bound, [source](#stmt-exp-fourier)): Let $C$ denote conditioned data. Conditional on $C$, let $U\in\mathbb F_2^t$ and $V\in\mathbb F_2^m$ be independent uniform vectors, independent also of auxiliary data $W$. For $1\leq a\leq q$ let $M_a:\mathbb F_2^t\to\mathbb F_2^m$ be linear, let $o_a=o_a(C,W)$, and let $f_a(C,z)$ be complex functions that do not depend separately on $U,V,W$. Set $$F=\frac1q\sum_a f_a(C,V+M_aU+o_a),\qquad \mu(C)=\frac1q\sum_a2^{-m}\sum_z f_a(C,z).$$ Suppose that for every nonzero linear functional $\zeta:\mathbb F_2^m\to\mathbb F_2$, every class of indices on which $\zeta M_a$ is the same has size at most $\theta q$. Then $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb E(F\mid C,W,U)&=\mu(C),\label{exp:conditional-mean}\\ \mathbb E\|F-\mathbb E(F\mid C)\|^2 &\leq\theta\,\mathbb E\left[ \frac1q\sum_a2^{-m}\sum_z\|f_a(C,z)\|^2\right]. \label{exp:conditional-variance} \end{aligned}$$ The conclusion applies with $\theta=10^{-3}$ for a full active $s$-block and with $\theta=0.55$ for any nonempty active initial part, using the coefficient multiplicity bounds of Lemma <a href="#tow:coefficient-choice" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tow:coefficient-choice">5</a>.
  - Explanation: Fourier orthogonality converts a multiplicity bound on induced functionals into a conditional variance bound without an additional conditional-mean error.
- **claim-uniform-mean-incidence-gap** (bound, [source](#stmt-exp-mean-gap)): For every height pair, and every fixed choice of its lower-cutoff tables, the mean incidence $M$ satisfies $$\big\\|M:\mathbf1_B^\perp\longrightarrow \mathbf1_A^\perp\big\\| \leq\sqrt{16\cdot10^{-3}+0.55}<0.8.$$ The bound is uniform in the number of full blocks, the final active length $1\leq t\leq s$, and $0\leq\alpha,\beta\leq2$.
  - Explanation: The mean incidence contracts centered functions uniformly over heights, lower tables and terminal block configurations.
- **claim-cut-lower-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-exp-cut-tail)): With lower tables fixed, for every nonempty $Z$ of volume at most $E$, $$\mathbb P\{b(Z)<v(Z)/20\}\leq\exp(-v(Z)/400).$$
  - Explanation: A cut with insufficient crossing edges has exponentially small probability in its degree volume.
- **claim-realized-incidence-gap** (construction, [source](#stmt-exp-realized-gap)): For all sufficiently large $\ell$, independently of the height pair and of its previously fixed lower tables, its current tables can be chosen so that <a href="#exp:actual-cut" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="exp:actual-cut">[exp:actual-cut]</a> holds. For this choice, the incidence operator of the actual $Y^0$ block, with each entry normalized by $\sqrt{dD'}$ in counting Hilbert spaces, sends normalized constants to normalized constants, as does its adjoint, and has norm at most $1-\chi$ between their orthogonal complements, where $$\chi=\frac1{800}.$$
  - Explanation: Current tables can be selected to satisfy all cut inequalities, yielding a uniform centered incidence contraction.
- **claim-padded-prefix-recovery** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prune-prefix-recovery)): Suppose two nonbacktracking paths in $Y^\#$ have the same oriented label word. At corresponding $A$-positions of radius at least $2j+1$, both padded streams agree through length $r+sj$, for every $j\geq0$. At corresponding $B$-positions of radius at least $2j$, both padded streams agree through length $sj$, for every $j\geq1$. The compared positions need not be in the same layer.
  - Explanation: Matching path words force agreement of padded stream prefixes at interior positions, even across different layers.
- **claim-short-cycle-support-count** (bound, [source](#stmt-prune-cycle-count)): Fix a layer and a side. A nonempty cyclically reduced oriented label word is the word of at most one based cyclically nonbacktracking closed path with its base on that layer and side. Consequently the number of vertices of $A_i$, or of $B_i$, supporting such a closed path of length at most $g_i$ is at most $$\sum_{a=1}^{\lfloor g_i\rfloor}(2m_\ell)^a.$$ For all sufficiently large $\ell$, this is a fraction at most $2^{-0.9H_i}$ of either vertex set, uniformly in $i$.
  - Explanation: A cyclically reduced word determines at most one based closed path on a fixed side and layer, making short-cycle support sparse.
- **claim-pruned-tower-properties** (construction, [source](#stmt-prune-tower-properties)): For all sufficiently large $\ell$, the preceding choices give a graph $Y^0$ on sets of sizes $\|A_i\|=d2^{H_i}$ and $\|B_i\|=2^{H_i}$, and its induced subgraph $Y$, with these properties.* 1. *For every $\|i-k\|\leq1$, the $Y^0$-block between $A_i$ and $B_k$ has degrees $d,D_{ik}$ with $D_{ik}=D2^{\|i\|-\|k\|}$. Its counting incidence divided by $\sqrt{dD_{ik}}$ maps normalized constants to normalized constants, as does its adjoint. On their orthogonal complements its norm is at most $1-\chi$, with the absolute constant $\chi$ of Proposition <a href="#exp:realized-gap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="exp:realized-gap">10</a>.* 2. *The fraction removed from any $A_i$ or $B_i$ is at most $\varepsilon_\ell$ of <a href="#prune:total-budget" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="prune:total-budget">[prune:total-budget]</a>.* 3. *The labels of $Y$ are folded, with positive alphabet size at most $m_\ell=O(d2^r(1+s)^2)$.* 4. *Every nonbacktracking path in $Y$ beginning at layer $i$ and having length at least $L_i$ is determined by its oriented word among all paths in $Y$, including paths beginning in other layers.* 5. *There is no nonempty cyclically nonbacktracking closed path in $Y$, based at layer $i$, of length at most $g_i$.
  - Explanation: The survivor retains small deletion loss, folded labels, long-word uniqueness and short-cycle exclusion, while expansion remains a property of the auxiliary tower.
- **claim-constant-space-deletion-loss** (bound, [source](#stmt-prune-constant-compression)): Let $\Pi$ be the orthogonal projection that retains the vertices of $Y$ in the counting Hilbert space of the full tower, with any additional Hilbert-space tensor factor. Let $C$ be the subspace of vectors that are constant on each separate $A_i$ and $B_i$ in the vertex coordinate. Then $$\\|(1-\Pi)\|_C\\|\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon_\ell}.$$ In particular the same bound holds on every subspace of $C$, uniformly in the additional tensor factor.
  - Explanation: Deleting a uniformly small fraction of each vertex set causes only the square-root loss on layerwise constant spaces, including tensor amplifications.
- **claim-cover-geometry-and-torsion** (construction, [source](#stmt-geo-main)): There are absolute constants $C_*,C_\bullet$ such that, for sufficiently large $\ell$, the preceding hypotheses have the following consequences. Every component map $\phi:C\to X$ is an embedding. Index copies by pairs $(C,\phi)$ modulo the equivalence $$(C,\phi)\sim (\delta_a C,\phi\delta_a^{-1}),\qquad a\in F.$$ Thus an indexed copy $Q$ remembers its source component and source vertices up to this deck alignment, in addition to its embedded image. There is a well-order of these copies and an induced intrinsic ball $B(Q)$ in each $Q$ such that* 1. *every vertex of $Q$ shared with an earlier copy belongs to $B(Q)$;* 2. *the subgraph of $Q$ consisting of all edges with at least one endpoint in $B(Q)$ is a forest;* 3. *for every vertex of $B(Q)$ lying over layer $i$, $$\|V(B(Q))\|\le\mathfrak w_i, \qquad \mathfrak w_i=2^{C_\bullet H_i}; \label{geo:weight}$$* 4. *if $D_Q$ is $V(Q)$ minus its vertices shared with earlier copies, the domains $D_Q$ are pairwise disjoint, and deleting every edge of $Q$ whose two endpoints lie in $D_Q$, for all $Q$, leaves a forest in $X$.* *Furthermore $\Gamma_{\mathrm{proj}}$ is torsion-free. None of the constants depends on $F$, the voltage values, the number of components of $P$, or $\|S\|$.
  - Explanation: Under the cover girth and labeling hypotheses, component embeddings admit controlled earlier overlaps and disjoint domains with a residual forest; the presented group is torsion-free.
- **claim-minimal-diagram-reductions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-reductions)): In either minimum diagram the following statements hold.* 1. *No bounded face has a freely trivial label word.* 2. *No edge separating two distinct bounded faces has aligned source incidences. Nor does an edge between a bounded face and an adjustable exterior side have aligned source incidences.* 3. *There is no unmarked vertex of degree one, and no bridge having both incidences on a bounded face.* *Here alignment means that, when both incidences are read in the same direction, a left deck transformation identifies their source edges.
  - Explanation: Minimal diagrams exclude trivial bounded faces, aligned source incidences across reducible contacts and removable leaves or bridges.
- **claim-short-arcs-euler-budget** (bound, [source](#stmt-geo-arcs)): Every arc incident with a bounded face has length less than $L_i$, when read from an endpoint in layer $i$ on that face. Its length is also less than $L_i$ on any adjustable-side incidence with which it is compared. Every bounded face has at least twenty arc incidences. If $b$ is the number of arcs and there are at most $m$ marked vertices, then $$b\le 3J+3m-3,\qquad 20J\le 2b,\qquad b\le 6m. \label{geo:euler}$$ These statements require only $C_*\ge10^4$ and sufficiently large $\ell$.
  - Explanation: Long-word uniqueness forces short arcs, the cover girth forces many incidences per bounded face, and Euler counting bounds the diagram's arc budget.
- **claim-component-embeddings** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-embeddings)): All component label maps $C\to X$ are injective on vertices and formal edges. The indexed copies of Theorem <a href="#geo:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geo:main">15</a> are therefore defined.
  - Explanation: Minimal failure diagrams cannot exist, so source components embed on vertices and formal edges.
- **claim-short-chain-fillings** (bound, [source](#stmt-geo-chain-filling)): In $\widehat X$ the integral oriented chain of any edge loop of length $n$ is a sum of at most $12n$ oriented chains of edge loops of length at most four.
  - Explanation: Loop chains in the coned graph decompose into a linearly bounded number of short loop chains.
- **claim-thin-triangles-from-fillings** (bound, [source](#stmt-geo-thin)): Let a connected graph with unit edge lengths have the conclusion of Lemma <a href="#geo:chain-filling" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geo:chain-filling">19</a>. Every geodesic triangle on its vertices is $D_0$-thin, where one may take $D_0=10^6$. Thinness here measures distance to the vertices on the other two sides.
  - Explanation: The short-chain filling inequality implies uniformly thin geodesic triangles without a local-finiteness assumption.
- **claim-center-avoiding-intrinsic-distance** (bound, [source](#stmt-geo-avoidance)): Fix a positive integer $L$. For sufficiently large $\ell$, uniformly in $Q$ and $i$, if a path in $\widehat X$ of length at most $L$ joins $u,x\in Q$, with $u$ in layer $i$, and avoids $c_Q$, then $$d_Q(u,x)\le24(L+1)L_i. \label{geo:avoidance-bound}$$ Here the threshold for $\ell$ can depend on $L$, which will subsequently be an absolute constant.
  - Explanation: A bounded ambient path avoiding the copy's own center bounds intrinsic distance in that copy at the local recovery scale.
- **claim-common-root-overlap-balls** (construction, [source](#stmt-geo-balls)): There is a well-order of the indexed copies for which the first three assertions of Theorem <a href="#geo:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geo:main">15</a> hold.
  - Explanation: Ordering centers by distance from a common root places all earlier intersections with each copy inside a single controlled intrinsic ball.
- **claim-direct-cycle-coordinates** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-cycle-directness)): For every field $k$, inclusion of the indexed copies induces an isomorphism of vector spaces $$\bigoplus_Q Z_1(Q;k)\ \longrightarrow\ Z_1(X;k), \qquad (c_Q)_Q\longmapsto\sum_Q c_Q. \label{geo:cycle-decomposition}$$ The direct sum is algebraic, so every tuple used has finite support.
  - Explanation: Every finite-support ambient cycle decomposes uniquely into finitely many indexed-copy cycles over any field.
- **claim-disjoint-domains-residual-forest** (construction, [source](#stmt-geo-forest)): Put $$D_Q=V(Q)\setminus\bigcup_{R<Q}V(R),\qquad E_{\mathrm{int}}=\bigcup_Q \{e\in E(Q):\text{ both endpoints of }e\text{ lie in }D_Q\}.$$ The $D_Q$ are pairwise disjoint, $V(Q)\setminus D_Q\subseteq V(B(Q))$, and the formal graph $(V(X),E(X)\setminus E_{\mathrm{int}})$ is a forest. Each edge in $E_{\mathrm{int}}$ belongs to the displayed set for exactly one $Q$.
  - Explanation: Assigning each shared vertex to its earliest copy gives disjoint domains, and removing their internal formal edges leaves a forest.
- **claim-equivariant-incidence-bijection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-incidences)): Let $$\mathcal I=\{(Q,x):Q\text{ an indexed copy},\ x\in V(Q)\}.$$ There is a canonical bijection $$\mathcal I\longrightarrow\Gamma_{\mathrm{proj}}\times V(Y),\qquad (Q,x)\longmapsto(x,\operatorname{type}_Q(x)), \label{geo:incidence-bijection}$$ where the type is the base vertex under the unique source vertex of the embedding. It intertwines left translation of $\Gamma_{\mathrm{proj}}$ with left translation of the first coordinate, with the base type fixed. In particular, for a fixed $x\in\Gamma_{\mathrm{proj}}$ and $v\in V(Y)$ there is exactly one incidence at $x$ of type $v$, even when $P$ is disconnected.
  - Explanation: Indexed-copy incidences are canonically identified with group vertices paired with base types, including disconnected voltage covers.
- **claim-copy-stabilizers-torsion-free** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-stabilizers)): The stabilizer in $\Gamma_{\mathrm{proj}}$ of an indexed copy embeds into the subgroup of $F$ preserving any representative source component. In particular that stabilizer has no nonidentity finite-order element.
  - Explanation: An indexed-copy stabilizer injects into the torsion-free deck group and therefore contains no nonidentity finite-order element.
- **claim-acyclic-cone-complex** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-acyclic)): For every field $k$, the augmented cellular chain complex $$0\longrightarrow C_2(Z;k)\xrightarrow{\partial_2}C_1(Z;k) \xrightarrow{\partial_1}C_0(Z;k)\xrightarrow{\varepsilon}k \longrightarrow0 \label{geo:acyclic-complex}$$ is exact.
  - Explanation: The cone complex has an exact augmented cellular chain complex over every field, using finite-support chains.
- **claim-presented-group-torsion-free** (theorem, [source](#stmt-geo-torsion)): The finitely generated group $\Gamma_{\mathrm{proj}}$ in <a href="#geo:presentation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="geo:presentation">[geo:presentation]</a> is torsion-free.
  - Explanation: A hypothetical finite cyclic subgroup would contradict the exact cone resolution through its periodic group resolution.
- **claim-metric-graph-rank-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-nil-rank)): Let $S$ be a finite connected metric graph with positive edge lengths, total length at most $Cg$, and girth greater than $g>0$. Girth means the infimum of lengths of embedded circles, with value $+\infty$ for a tree. Put $$B(C)=\max\{1,\lceil20C\rceil\}^{\,2}.$$ Then $b_1(S)\le B(C)$. Vertices of degree one and parallel edges are allowed.
  - Explanation: A finite metric graph with controlled length relative to girth has uniformly bounded first Betti number.
- **claim-bounded-word-matrix-detector** (construction, [source](#stmt-nil-word)): Let $W$ be a nonempty cyclically nonbacktracking closed walk in a unit-edge graph of girth greater than $g$, with length at most $Cg$. There is a spanning tree of its support such that, after its edge variables are set equal to the identity, the holonomy word has a nontrivial free reduction of length at most $$L(C)=\lceil3CB(C)\rceil.$$ If $m>L(C)$, that word is not an identity on the group $U_{\mathbb Z}$ of upper unitriangular $m$-by-$m$ integer matrices. Every entry of any word in generic upper unitriangular matrices and their inverses is a polynomial of total degree at most $m-1$ in their upper entries. Nonidentity is preserved if each edge variable is multiplied on either side by a fixed upper unitriangular matrix.
  - Explanation: Collapsing a suitable spanning tree leaves a bounded nontrivial word detected by upper unitriangular integer matrices; generic entries have uniformly bounded polynomial degree.
- **claim-integral-graded-affine-groups** (construction, [source](#stmt-nil-grading)): The laws above are polynomial with integer coefficients in $(\lambda,b-1,z)$. Give the coordinates weights $$\operatorname{wt}(b-1)_{ij}=j-i,\quad \operatorname{wt}\lambda_{ij}=m-(j-i),\quad \operatorname{wt}z=m.$$ Scaling weight $a$ by $y^a$ defines an automorphism $\Delta_y$, for $y>0$, and $c(\Delta_y\gamma,\Delta_y\eta)=y^m c(\gamma,\eta)$. Lebesgue coordinate measure is both left and right Haar measure on $E$ and $\bar E$; inversion preserves it. The Jacobian of $\Delta_y$ on $\bar E$ is $y^{mn_{\mathrm f}}$. The integer coordinate sets $E_{\mathbb Z}$ and $\bar E_{\mathbb Z}$ are countable nilpotent torsion-free groups.
  - Explanation: The affine group laws respect a positive integral grading and coordinate Haar measure, and their integer points are nilpotent and torsion-free.
- **claim-equivariant-trace-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-nil-trace)): Let $L$ be a countable group with a right action as above and let $I,J$ be finite or countable auxiliary sets. On $\mathcal H_I=\ell^2(L)\otimes\ell^2(I)$, define, for equivariant positive bounded $B$, $$\operatorname{Tr}_I(B) =\sum_{i\in I}\langle B\delta_{(1,i)},\delta_{(1,i)}\rangle \in[0,\infty].$$ For a bounded equivariant $A:\mathcal H_I\to\mathcal H_J$, $$\label{nil:adjoint-square} \operatorname{Tr}_I(A^*A)=\operatorname{Tr}_J(AA^*).$$ This trace is additive on positive operators, monotone, and faithful on projections, and does not depend on the chosen orthonormal basis of the auxiliary Hilbert space. Equivariantly equivalent projections have equal size. A bounded equivariant map injective on the range of a projection $P$, with image there contained in the range of $Q$, gives $\operatorname{Tr}(P)\le\operatorname{Tr}(Q)$. These statements apply to countable orthogonal sums and to each Fourier fiber. Integrating a fiber trace gives the identity trace before Fourier transformation.
  - Explanation: The identity trace obeys the adjoint-square identity, faithful projection comparison and compatibility with countable sums and Fourier integration.
- **claim-schwartz-twisted-idempotents** (construction, [source](#stmt-nil-schwartz)): For each sign $\epsilon$, there is a Schwartz function $J_\epsilon$ on $\bar E$ with $$J_\epsilon^{*_\epsilon}=J_\epsilon,\qquad J_\epsilon *_\epsilon J_\epsilon=J_\epsilon,\qquad \\|J_\epsilon\\|_{L^2(\bar E)}=1.$$
  - Explanation: Gaussian rank-one kernels yield self-adjoint Schwartz idempotents for the twisted convolution operations.
- **claim-weighted-riemann-approximation** (bound, [source](#stmt-nil-riemann)): For positive integer weights $w_1,\ldots,w_k$, set $$\mathcal L_y=\prod_{a=1}^k y^{w_a}\mathbb Z,\qquad v_y=y^{w_1+\cdots+w_k},\qquad 0<y\le1.$$ For Schwartz $f$ on $\mathbb R^k$, $$v_y\sum_{a\in\mathcal L_y}f(a)\longrightarrow \int_{\mathbb R^k}f(x)\,dx.$$ The corresponding normalized sums of absolute values are uniformly bounded. For jointly Schwartz $G(a,h)$ on $\mathbb R^k\times\mathbb R^k$, and every $M$, $$\label{nil:riemann-weighted} \sup_h(1+\|h\|)^M \left\|v_y\sum_{a\in\mathcal L_y}G(a,h) -\int G(a,h)\,da\right\|\le C_{G,M}y.$$
  - Explanation: Weighted rectangular lattice sums converge for Schwartz functions and satisfy uniform weighted error estimates for jointly Schwartz kernels.
- **claim-nonzero-discrete-twisted-projections** (construction, [source](#stmt-nil-projection)): There are constants $\theta_0>0,C<\infty$, depending only on $m$ and the two fixed Schwartz functions, with the following properties. For $0<\|\theta\|<\theta_0$, set $$\epsilon=\operatorname{sign}\theta,\quad y=\|\theta\|^{1/m},\quad \nu=y^{mn_{\mathrm f}}=\|\theta\|^{n_{\mathrm f}},$$ and define the norm-convergent series $$\label{nil:K} K_\theta=\sum_{\gamma\in L} \nu J_\epsilon(\Delta_y\gamma)u_\theta(\gamma).$$ It is self-adjoint and satisfies, uniformly for both signs, $$\begin{aligned} \\|K_\theta\\|_{\mathrm{coeff},1}&\le C,& \\|K_\theta\\|_{\mathrm{coeff},2}&\asymp\sqrt{\nu}, \label{nil:Knorms}\\ \\|K_\theta^2-K_\theta\\|_{\mathrm{coeff},1}&\le Cy,& \\|K_\theta^2-K_\theta\\|_{\mathrm{coeff},2}&\le Cy\sqrt{\nu}. \label{nil:defects} \end{aligned}$$ Continuous functional calculus in $C_r^*(L,\theta c)$ gives a projection $p_\theta$ with $$\label{nil:projection-bounds} \\|p_\theta-K_\theta\\|\le Cy,\qquad 0<\operatorname{tr}_\theta(p_\theta)\le C\|\theta\|^{n_{\mathrm f}}.$$ These fields are measurable on the intervals $0<\|\theta\|<\theta_0$.
  - Explanation: Sampling the continuous idempotents gives small coefficient defects; functional calculus produces nonzero discrete twisted projections with controlled identity trace.
- **claim-compact-template-stability** (bound, [source](#stmt-nil-templates)): The functions $J_\epsilon$ in Equation <a href="#nil:K" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="nil:K">[nil:K]</a> may be replaced by smooth compactly supported $J_\epsilon^\flat$ at arbitrarily small uniform coefficient $\ell^1$ cost for sufficiently small $\|\theta\|$. For fixed such a function, replacing $\alpha>0$ by $\beta>0$, where $\|\log(\alpha/\beta)\|\le a\le1$, changes the coefficients $$\alpha^{n_{\mathrm f}} J_\epsilon^\flat(\Delta_{\alpha^{1/m}}\gamma)$$ by $\ell^1$ amount at most $Ca$, uniformly for sufficiently small $\alpha,\beta$. These coefficient bounds imply operator bounds when evaluated using any family of unitaries.
  - Explanation: Compact cutoffs and small relative scale changes incur uniformly small coefficient errors, hence operator errors in arbitrary unitary evaluations.
- **claim-finite-factor-twisted-absorption** (theorem, [source](#stmt-nil-absorption)): On the second quotient factor, define $$V_t(\gamma)=u_t(\gamma)\otimes R_\gamma.$$ Its multiplier is $\mathrm e((t-\delta)c)$, and it is unitarily equivalent to $N$ copies of $u_{t-\delta}$. The equivalence fixes the quotient-identity fiber, preserving the identity trace with its unnormalized matrix sum. Thus every coefficient sum, and every continuous spectral function of it, has the same operator norm as its evaluation in $u_{t-\delta}$; the identity trace of a projection is multiplied by $N$.
  - Explanation: The finite magnetic representation shifts the effective twist while preserving the identity block and retaining the unnormalized matrix multiplicity.
- **claim-smooth-moving-layer-windows** (construction, [source](#stmt-nil-window)): There is a smooth nonnegative $h$, supported in $(-1,1)$ and positive on $[-0.7,0.7]$, with $\sum_{i\in\mathbb Z}h(v-i)^2=1$ for every $v\in\mathbb R$. For $0<t<\delta$, set $$\label{nil:h} b(t)=\kappa^{-1}\log_2\frac{t}{\delta-t},\qquad h_i(t)=h(b(t)-i),$$ and extend $h_i$ by zero to the rest of the circle. Each extension is smooth. The active set $J(t)=\{i:h_i(t)\ne0\}$ has one or two elements, adjacent if two, and $\sum_i h_i(t)^2=1$ on the open interval. With $w_i=q^{-1}2^{-\kappa\|i\|}$, its support length and derivatives satisfy $$\|\operatorname{supp}h_i\|\le C_\kappa w_i,\qquad \\|\partial_t^j h_i\\|_\infty\le C_{\kappa,j}w_i^{-j}.$$ On an active index $i$, uniformly in $\ell,i,t$, $$\begin{aligned} \log_2(t^{-1}) &=\log_2q+\kappa\max(-i,0)+O_\kappa(1),\label{nil:log-t}\\ \log_2((\delta-t)^{-1}) &=\log_2q+\kappa\max(i,0)+O_\kappa(1),\label{nil:log-tau}\\ t(\delta-t)&\le 2^\kappa q^{-2}2^{-\kappa\|i\|}. \label{nil:product} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: A smooth square partition of unity activates at most adjacent layers and controls support scales, derivatives and endpoint decay.
- **claim-weighted-fiber-trace-decay** (bound, [source](#stmt-nil-weighted)): For $\mathfrak w_i=2^{C_\bullet H_i}$, define $$\mu_\ell=\sup_{\substack{0<t<\delta\\i\in J(t)}} \mathfrak w_i\,s_p(t).$$ Then $\mu_\ell\to0$ as $\ell\to\infty$. More explicitly, $$\label{nil:weighted-estimate} \mathfrak w_i N t^{n_{\mathrm f}}(\delta-t)^{n_{\mathrm f}} \le C d^{-(A_*n_{\mathrm f}-C_\bullet h_*)} 2^{-(\kappa n_{\mathrm f}-C_\bullet)\|i\|} \qquad(i\in J(t)).$$ The two exponents subtracted on the right are positive.
  - Explanation: The product of geometric layer weight and fiber projection trace vanishes uniformly under the prescribed parameter hierarchy.
- **claim-model-upper-band** (construction, [source](#stmt-spec-model)): For $0<t<\delta$, define an isometry on $p(t)\mathcal H_t$ by $$V_*(t)\zeta=2^{-1/2} \left(\bigoplus_i h_i(t)a_i\otimes\zeta\right) \oplus 2^{-1/2}\left(\bigoplus_i h_i(t)b_i\otimes\zeta\right), \qquad E_*(t)=V_*(t)V_*(t)^*. \label{spec:model-isometry}$$ Then $O(t)E_*(t)=E_*(t)$, $\\|O(t)\\|\leq1$, and $$O(t)\|_{(1-E_*(t))\mathcal H^0_t}\leq(1-\chi)I, \qquad \operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{id}}E_*(t) =\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{id}}p(t). \label{spec:model-gap-trace}$$ Set $E_*(t)=0$ outside the window; the same upper bound holds there. Furthermore $$\\|(1-\Pi)E_*(t)\\|\leq\sqrt{\beta_\ell} \label{spec:model-cut}$$ uniformly in $t$.
  - Explanation: A symmetric combination of layer constants carries the model's upper eigenvalue; centered directions have a uniform gap and the upper trace equals the transverse trace.
- **claim-simultaneous-voltage-weight-realization** (construction, [source](#stmt-sam-realization)): For every fixed $\eta>0$, and for all sufficiently large $\ell$, there are voltages $z_e\in F$ and matrices $W_e\in M_N(\mathbb C)$ on the positively oriented edges of $Y$ with the following properties.* 1. *There is a constant $C_\eta$, independent of $\ell$ and the layer, such that $\\|W_e\\|\le C_\eta$. Augmenting an original tower label by its matrix weight gives a finite positive alphabet of size $o(dD)$. The normalizing number $D_{ik}$ is determined by the original part of the label.* 2. *The voltage cover has no nonempty cyclically nonbacktracking closed walk based in layer $i$ of length at most $C_*H_i/\log_2d$.* 3. *Let $T_P$, extended by zero on the deleted vertex indices, have entries from $B_k$ to $A_i$ $$\label{sam:entries} (T_P)_{ab}= \frac1{\sqrt{dD_{ik}}}\sum_{e:a\to b}W_e\rho(z_e), \qquad a\in A_i,\quad b\in B_k,$$ with the adjoint entries in the opposite direction. Then $$\label{sam:approx} \\|T_P-\Pi O\Pi\\|\le\eta.$$ Both operators have layer width one. In particular, their central Fourier fields satisfy the same inequality almost everywhere, including $\\|T_P(t)\\|\le\eta$ outside the open window.* *The matrices $W_e$ are the actual weights of the individual edges of the voltage cover, so $T_P$ reduces on each component of that cover.
  - Explanation: A common edge assignment approximates the compressed model in the full regular norm, enforces cover girth and keeps the matrix-weight alphabet finite and sufficiently small.
- **claim-finite-box-regular-norm-test** (bound, [source](#stmt-sam-boxes)): Let $x\ge2$. Suppose a finite set of elements of $F$, including their inverses, has every integral coordinate bounded by $x^a$, where $a$ is fixed. For every fixed $B>0$ there is a finite coordinate box $\mathcal B_x\subset F$ with $$\|\mathcal B_x\|\le x^{c},\qquad \frac{\|\mathcal B_x\mathbin\triangle\mathcal B_x g\|} {\|\mathcal B_x\|}\le Cx^{-B}$$ for all the specified elements $g$. Constants $c,C$ depend on $a,B,F$, but not on the number of specified elements or on $x$.* *Let $A$ be a self-adjoint finite matrix of right convolution sums on $\ell^2(J)\otimes\ell^2(F)\otimes\mathbb C^n$, where $J$ is a finite index set, with these group supports. Write its coefficients as $C_{uv}(g)\in M_n(\mathbb C)$, and suppose $$\max_{u\in J}\sum_{v\in J}\sum_g\\|C_{uv}(g)\\|\le R.$$ If $P_x$ retains the deck indices in $\mathcal B_x$ and all the other indices, then $$\label{sam:finite-test} \\|P_xAP_x\\|\le\\|A\\| \le\\|P_xAP_x\\|+Cx^{-B}R.$$ The same conclusion holds for a rectangular convolution matrix after self-adjoint off-diagonal doubling, using the larger of its absolute row and column sum bounds. The constants do not depend on the matrix amplification $n$.
  - Explanation: Graded coordinate boxes are nearly invariant under bounded supports and turn a finite compression into a two-sided regular-norm estimate uniform in matrix amplification.
- **claim-empirical-regular-norm-approximation** (construction, [source](#stmt-sam-empirical)): Set $M=\lfloor d^{1/32}\rfloor$, with $q=d^{A_*}$ and $A_*$ fixed. Consider at most $C(1+\log q)$ finite distributions of operators $W\rho(g)$ on $\ell^2(F)\otimes\mathbb C^N$. Suppose $\\|W\\|\le B_0$, every possible group coordinate is bounded by $q^a$, and $N\le q^a$, for fixed constants $a,B_0,C$. For each fixed $\varepsilon>0$, and sufficiently large $\ell$, each expectation has an $M$-term empirical average within $\varepsilon$ in the full regular norm. The averages may be chosen for all the distributions simultaneously.
  - Explanation: Finite empirical lists simultaneously approximate the prescribed operator expectations in the full regular norm under fixed support and norm bounds.
- **claim-no-singleton-closed-walk-count** (bound, [source](#stmt-sam-walk-count)): Let a finite graph have maximum degree $\Delta\ge1$. Fix a vertex $v$ and an integer $l\ge1$. Suppose the support of every closed walk under consideration has first Betti number at most $b$. The number of length-$2l$ closed walks from $v$ in which no undirected edge occurs exactly once is at most $$C_b^l\Delta^l.$$ Multiple edges, if present, are treated as distinct edges.
  - Explanation: A bounded-rank core and tree-excursion encoding control closed walks without singleton edges, treating parallel edges distinctly.
- **claim-upper-space-compression-stability** (bound, [source](#stmt-spec-compression)): Let $A=A^*$ on a Hilbert space, with $\\|A\\|\leq2$, and let $E$ be a reducing projection such that $$\\|(A-I)E\\|\leq a,\qquad A\|_{(1-E)\mathcal H}\leq(1-b)I,\qquad 0<b<1.$$ Let $J$ be an orthogonal projection with $\varepsilon:=\\|(1-J)E\\|\leq1/2$. The operator $JE$ has closed range. Writing $E^J$ for the projection onto that range, put, on $J\mathcal H$, $$B_J=E^J+(J-E^J)A(J-E^J). \label{spec:compression-model}$$ Then $$\\|JAJ-B_J\\|\leq a+20\varepsilon, \qquad B_J\|_{(J-E^J)\mathcal H}\leq(1-b)I, \qquad \\|B_J\\|\leq2. \label{spec:compression-error}$$ There is a partial isometry $V$ with $V^*V=E$, $VV^*=E^J$, and $\\|V-E\\|\leq2\varepsilon$. If $A,E,J$ commute with a specified unitary or projective unitary action, then so do all the projections and maps just constructed. In particular, in a setting with the identity trace of Lemma <a href="#nil:trace" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="nil:trace">32</a>, $E$ and $E^J$ have equal identity traces. These statements include $E=0$.
  - Explanation: Small deletion from a reducing upper space preserves a closed compressed range, controlled spectral comparison and an equivariant trace-preserving partial isometry.
- **claim-contour-projection-perturbation** (bound, [source](#stmt-spec-perturbation)): Suppose $B=B^*$ has a reducing projection $F$ with $BF=F$, and its complementary spectrum is contained in $[-2,1-b]$, where $0<b<1$. Let $r_0>0$ satisfy $r_0\leq b/2$ and $r_0<1$. If $A=A^*$ and $\\|A-B\\|\leq u<r_0$, the circle $\mathcal C_{r_0}=\{z:\|z-1\|=r_0\}$ lies in the resolvent sets of both operators. The enclosed spectral projection $F_A$ satisfies $$\\|F_A-F\\|\leq\frac{u}{r_0-u}. \label{spec:projection-distance}$$ Its enclosed spectrum lies in $[1-u,1+u]$; all the other spectrum of $A$ is at most $1-b+u$. If $u<r_0/2$, the projections $F_A$ and $F$ are equivalent by a partial isometry belonging to the algebra of bounded operators generated by them. This equivalence preserves any common equivariance and its identity trace.
  - Explanation: A norm perturbation smaller than the contour radius preserves spectral separation; a stronger bound gives equivalent upper projections and equal equivariant traces.
- **claim-cover-band-exact-fiber-trace** (construction, [source](#stmt-spec-cover-projection)): The operator $T_P$ has a separated upper spectral projection $E_P$, obtained by integration around $\mathcal C_{r_0}$. It satisfies $$\\|T_P\\|\leq2,\qquad \\|(T_P-I)E_P\\|\leq\alpha,\qquad T_P\|_{(1-E_P)\mathcal H}\leq(1-\chi+\alpha)I. \label{spec:cover-gap}$$ In the central direct integral, for almost every $t$, $$\operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{id}}E_P(t) =\begin{cases} \operatorname{tr}_{\mathrm{id}}p(t),&0<t<\delta,\\ 0,&t\notin(0,\delta). \end{cases} \label{spec:exact-fiber-trace}$$ The projection $E_P$ is nonzero and reduces to the connected components of $P$. Its diagonal at a lifted vertex depends only on the base vertex.
  - Explanation: The actual voltage-cover operator has a nonzero separated upper projection with exactly the model's fiber trace and deck-invariant diagonals.
- **claim-weighted-cover-localization** (bound, [source](#stmt-spec-localization)): For the fixed choice of $\eta$ in Equation <a href="#spec:eta-choice" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="spec:eta-choice">[spec:eta-choice]</a>, $$\mathcal S\leq96\mu_\ell=o(1). \label{spec:S-small}$$ In particular the bound contains no additional factor equal to a layer cardinality or to the matrix dimension already included in $\mu_\ell$.
  - Explanation: Layerwise resolvent propagation bounds the weighted projection trace without multiplying by layer cardinalities or adding matrix multiplicity.
- **claim-finite-ball-trimming-stability** (bound, [source](#stmt-spec-copy-cut)): Let $D_Q$ and $B(Q)$ be the assigned domain and trimming ball from Theorem <a href="#geo:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geo:main">15</a>. Then $$\\|\mathbf1_{B(Q)}E_Q\\|^2\leq\mathcal S. \label{spec:ball-HS}$$ For $\mathcal S\leq1/4$, the compression of $T_Q$ to $\ell^2(D_Q)\otimes\mathbb C^N$ is within $\alpha+20\sqrt{\mathcal S}$ of an operator $C_Q$ which is $1$ on a projection $F_Q$ equivalent to $E_Q$, has norm at most $2$, and is at most $1-\chi+\alpha$ on the complementary space. In particular $F_Q\ne0$ whenever $E_Q\ne0$.
  - Explanation: The weighted identity trace controls trimming-ball norm loss and preserves a nonzero equivalent local upper space after domain compression.
- **claim-matrix-forest-adjacency-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-spec-forest-norm)): Suppose a countable forest has maximum degree at most $m_1$, and a self-adjoint adjacency on it has matrix edge weights of norms at most $w_1$. Its norm is at most $2w_1\sqrt{m_1}$.
  - Explanation: Rooting each tree bounds matrix-weighted forest adjacency through degree and edge-weight bounds.
- **claim-global-reduced-spectral-gap** (construction, [source](#stmt-spec-global-gap)): For sufficiently large $\ell$, the operator in Equation <a href="#spec:Cayley-operator" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="spec:Cayley-operator">[spec:Cayley-operator]</a> satisfies $$\operatorname{spec}T \subset(-\infty,1-\chi+4\alpha]\, \cup[1-3\alpha,1+3\alpha]. \label{spec:global-spectrum}$$ The second spectral part is nonempty. Its projection $$p_0=\mathbf1_{[1-\chi/4,\infty)}(T) \in M_N(C_r^*(\Gamma_{\mathrm{proj}})) \label{spec:p0}$$ is consequently nonzero.
  - Explanation: The Cayley operator retains a nonempty separated upper band and therefore a nonzero projection in the matrix reduced group algebra.
- **claim-total-overlap-restriction-loss** (bound, [source](#stmt-spec-lost-restrictions)): Put $I_Q=V(Q)\setminus D_Q\subset B(Q)$. For every $\xi\in\mathcal H$, $$\sum_Q\\|E_Q(\xi\|_{I_Q})\\|^2\leq\mathcal S\\|\xi\\|^2, \label{spec:all-copy-cut-loss}$$ where restrictions are extended by zero inside their copies.
  - Explanation: The weighted incidence count pays for every trimming-ball factor in the summed local restriction loss.
- **claim-bounded-equivariant-restriction-map** (construction, [source](#stmt-spec-restriction)): The formula $$A\xi=\big(E_Q(\xi\|_{V(Q)})\big)_Q \quad(\xi\in\mathcal H) \label{spec:analysis-operator}$$ defines a bounded equivariant operator $A:\mathcal H\to\mathcal U$, with $\\|A\\|\leq1+\sqrt{\mathcal S}$. Its kernel $K$ is a closed invariant subspace, and every $\xi\in K$ satisfies $$E_Q(\xi\|_{V(Q)})=0\quad\text{for every indexed }Q. \label{spec:kernel-restrictions}$$ Moreover, $$\operatorname{Tr}_{\Gamma_{\mathrm{proj}}} P_{K^\perp}\leq\mathcal S_0. \label{spec:kernel-complement-size}$$
  - Explanation: Restriction to all local upper spaces is bounded and equivariant; the orthogonal complement of its kernel has small identity trace.
- **claim-global-upper-space-small-trace** (bound, [source](#stmt-spec-kernel-form)): With the choices in Equations <a href="#spec:S-final-choice" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="spec:S-final-choice">[spec:S-final-choice]</a> and <a href="#spec:forest-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="spec:forest-error">[spec:forest-error]</a>, $$\langle T\xi,\xi\rangle \leq(1-\chi+3\alpha)\\|\xi\\|^2 \quad(\xi\in K). \label{spec:kernel-form-bound}$$ Consequently the bounded equivariant map $P_{K^\perp}:p_0\mathcal H\to K^\perp$ is injective, and $$0<\tau_{\Gamma_{\mathrm{proj}},N}(p_0) \leq\mathcal S_0\leq\mathcal S<1/2. \label{spec:strict-trace}$$
  - Explanation: The quadratic-form ceiling on the restriction kernel makes its complement receive the global upper space injectively, bounding the unnormalized trace.
- **claim-final-matrix-projection** (construction, [source](#stmt-spec-matrix-projection)): For the fixed choices made above and some sufficiently large integer $\ell$, the construction defines a finitely generated torsion-free group $\Gamma_{\mathrm{proj}}$, a positive integer $N$, and the self-adjoint finite group-algebra matrix $T$ in Equation <a href="#spec:Cayley-operator" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="spec:Cayley-operator">[spec:Cayley-operator]</a>. Its spectrum in the reduced representation has the separation in Equation <a href="#spec:global-spectrum" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="spec:global-spectrum">[spec:global-spectrum]</a>. The resulting projection $p_0$ in Equation <a href="#spec:p0" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="spec:p0">[spec:p0]</a> belongs to $M_N(C_r^*(\Gamma_{\mathrm{proj}}))$ and obeys $$0<\sum_{j=1}^N\tau_{\Gamma_{\mathrm{proj}}}((p_0)_{jj})<\frac12.$$
  - Explanation: One sufficiently large finite parameter realizes finite generation, torsion-freeness, reduced spectral separation and the required strictly fractional unnormalized matrix trace simultaneously.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-exp-fourier",
        "exact_quote": "Let $C$ denote conditioned data. Conditional on $C$, let $U\\in\\mathbb F_2^t$ and $V\\in\\mathbb F_2^m$ be independent uniform vectors, independent also of auxiliary data $W$. For $1\\leq a\\leq q$ let $M_a:\\mathbb F_2^t\\to\\mathbb F_2^m$ be linear, let $o_a=o_a(C,W)$, and let $f_a(C,z)$ be complex functions that do not depend separately on $U,V,W$. Set $$F=\\frac1q\\sum_a f_a(C,V+M_aU+o_a),\\qquad\n \\mu(C)=\\frac1q\\sum_a2^{-m}\\sum_z f_a(C,z).$$ Suppose that for every nonzero linear functional $\\zeta:\\mathbb F_2^m\\to\\mathbb F_2$, every class of indices on which $\\zeta M_a$ is the same has size at most $\\theta q$. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb E(F\\mid C,W,U)&=\\mu(C),\\label{exp:conditional-mean}\\\\\n \\mathbb E|F-\\mathbb E(F\\mid C)|^2\n &\\leq\\theta\\,\\mathbb E\\left[\n    \\frac1q\\sum_a2^{-m}\\sum_z|f_a(C,z)|^2\\right].\n \\label{exp:conditional-variance}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The conclusion applies with $\\theta=10^{-3}$ for a full active $s$-block and with $\\theta=0.55$ for any nonempty active initial part, using the coefficient multiplicity bounds of Lemma <a href=\"#tow:coefficient-choice\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"tow:coefficient-choice\">5</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-285.2"
      },

Set $$F=\\frac1q\\sum_a f_a(C,V+M_aU+o_a),\\qquad\n \\mu(C)=\\frac1q\\sum_a2^{-m}\\sum_z f_a(C,z).$$ Suppose that for every nonzero linear functional $\\zeta:\\mathbb F_2^m\\to\\mathbb F_2$, every class of indices on which $\\zeta M_a$ is the same has size at most $\\theta q$. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb E(F\\mid C,W,U)&=\\mu(C),\\label{exp:conditional-mean}\\\\\n \\mathbb E|F-\\mathbb E(F\\mid C)|^2\n &\\leq\\theta\\,\\mathbb E\\left[\n    \\frac1q\\sum_a2^{-m}\\sum_z|f_a(C,z)|^2\\right].\n \\label{exp:conditional-variance}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The conclusion applies with $\\theta=10^{-3}$ for a full active $s$-block and with $\\theta=0.55$ for any nonempty active initial part, using the coefficient multiplicity bounds of Lemma <a href=\"#tow:coefficient-choice\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"tow:coefficient-choice\">5</a>.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prune-cycle-count",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a layer and a side. A nonempty cyclically reduced oriented label word is the word of at most one based cyclically nonbacktracking closed path with its base on that layer and side. Consequently the number of vertices of $A_i$, or of $B_i$, supporting such a closed path of length at most $g_i$ is at most $$\\sum_{a=1}^{\\lfloor g_i\\rfloor}(2m_\\ell)^a.$$ For all sufficiently large $\\ell$, this is a fraction at most $2^{-0.9H_i}$ of either vertex set, uniformly in $i$.",
        "source_id": "PM-285.2"
      },

        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prune-tower-properties",
        "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently large $\\ell$, the preceding choices give a graph $Y^0$ on sets of sizes $|A_i|=d2^{H_i}$ and $|B_i|=2^{H_i}$, and its induced subgraph $Y$, with these properties.*\n\n1.  *For every $|i-k|\\leq1$, the $Y^0$-block between $A_i$ and $B_k$ has degrees $d,D_{ik}$ with $D_{ik}=D2^{|i|-|k|}$. Its counting incidence divided by $\\sqrt{dD_{ik}}$ maps normalized constants to normalized constants, as does its adjoint. On their orthogonal complements its norm is at most $1-\\chi$, with the absolute constant $\\chi$ of Proposition <a href=\"#exp:realized-gap\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"exp:realized-gap\">10</a>.*\n\n2.  *The fraction removed from any $A_i$ or $B_i$ is at most $\\varepsilon_\\ell$ of <a href=\"#prune:total-budget\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prune:total-budget\">[prune:total-budget]</a>.*\n\n3.  *The labels of $Y$ are folded, with positive alphabet size at most $m_\\ell=O(d2^r(1+s)^2)$.*\n\n4.  *Every nonbacktracking path in $Y$ beginning at layer $i$ and having length at least $L_i$ is determined by its oriented word among all paths in $Y$, including paths beginning in other layers.*\n\n5.  *There is no nonempty cyclically nonbacktracking closed path in $Y$, based at layer $i$, of length at most $g_i$.",
        "source_id": "PM-285.2"
      },

On their orthogonal complements its norm is at most $1-\\chi$, with the absolute constant $\\chi$ of Proposition <a href=\"#exp:realized-gap\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"exp:realized-gap\">10</a>.*\n\n2.  *The fraction removed from any $A_i$ or $B_i$ is at most $\\varepsilon_\\ell$ of <a href=\"#prune:total-budget\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prune:total-budget\">[prune:total-budget]</a>.*\n\n3.  *The labels of $Y$ are folded, with positive alphabet size at most $m_\\ell=O(d2^r(1+s)^2)$.*\n\n4.  *Every nonbacktracking path in $Y$ beginning at layer $i$ and having length at least $L_i$ is determined by its oriented word among all paths in $Y$, including paths beginning in other layers.*\n\n5.  *There is no nonempty cyclically nonbacktracking closed path in $Y$, based at layer $i$, of length at most $g_i$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prune-constant-compression",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Pi$ be the orthogonal projection that retains the vertices of $Y$ in the counting Hilbert space of the full tower, with any additional Hilbert-space tensor factor. Then $$\\|(1-\\Pi)|_C\\|\\leq\\sqrt{\\varepsilon_\\ell}.$$ In particular the same bound holds on every subspace of $C$, uniformly in the additional tensor factor.",
        "source_id": "PM-285.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geo-main",
        "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $C_*,C_\\bullet$ such that, for sufficiently large $\\ell$, the preceding hypotheses have the following consequences. Every component map $\\phi:C\\to X$ is an embedding. Index copies by pairs $(C,\\phi)$ modulo the equivalence $$(C,\\phi)\\sim\n (\\delta_a C,\\phi\\delta_a^{-1}),\\qquad a\\in F.$$ Thus an indexed copy $Q$ remembers its source component and source vertices up to this deck alignment, in addition to its embedded image. There is a well-order of these copies and an induced intrinsic ball $B(Q)$ in each $Q$ such that*\n\n1.  *every vertex of $Q$ shared with an earlier copy belongs to $B(Q)$;*\n\n2.  *the subgraph of $Q$ consisting of all edges with at least one endpoint in $B(Q)$ is a forest;*\n\n3.  *for every vertex of $B(Q)$ lying over layer $i$, $$|V(B(Q))|\\le\\mathfrak w_i,\n     \\qquad \\mathfrak w_i=2^{C_\\bullet H_i};\n     \\label{geo:weight}$$*\n\n4.  *if $D_Q$ is $V(Q)$ minus its vertices shared with earlier copies, the domains $D_Q$ are pairwise disjoint, and deleting every edge of $Q$ whose two endpoints lie in $D_Q$, for all $Q$, leaves a forest in $X$.*\n\n*Furthermore $\\Gamma_{\\mathrm{proj}}$ is torsion-free. None of the constants depends on $F$, the voltage values, the number of components of $P$, or $|S|$.",
        "source_id": "PM-285.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geo-reductions",
        "exact_quote": "In either minimum diagram the following statements hold.*\n\n1.  *No bounded face has a freely trivial label word.*\n\n2.  *No edge separating two distinct bounded faces has aligned source incidences. Nor does an edge between a bounded face and an adjustable exterior side have aligned source incidences.*\n\n3.  *There is no unmarked vertex of degree one, and no bridge having both incidences on a bounded face.*\n\n*Here alignment means that, when both incidences are read in the same direction, a left deck transformation identifies their source edges.",
        "source_id": "PM-285.2"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-285.2"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geo-arcs",
        "exact_quote": "Every arc incident with a bounded face has length less than $L_i$, when read from an endpoint in layer $i$ on that face. If $b$ is the number of arcs and there are at most $m$ marked vertices, then $$b\\le 3J+3m-3,\\qquad 20J\\le 2b,\\qquad b\\le 6m.\n \\label{geo:euler}$$ These statements require only $C_*\\ge10^4$ and sufficiently large $\\ell$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-geo-incidences",
        "exact_quote": "Let $$\\mathcal I=\\{(Q,x):Q\\text{ an indexed copy},\\ x\\in V(Q)\\}.$$ There is a canonical bijection $$\\mathcal I\\longrightarrow\\Gamma_{\\mathrm{proj}}\\times V(Y),\\qquad\n (Q,x)\\longmapsto(x,\\operatorname{type}_Q(x)),\n \\label{geo:incidence-bijection}$$ where the type is the base vertex under the unique source vertex of the embedding.         "anchor": "stmt-nil-word",
        "exact_quote": "Let $W$ be a nonempty cyclically nonbacktracking closed walk in a unit-edge graph of girth greater than $g$, with length at most $Cg$. There is a spanning tree of its support such that, after its edge variables are set equal to the identity, the holonomy word has a nontrivial free reduction of length at most $$L(C)=\\lceil3CB(C)\\rceil.$$ If $m>L(C)$, that word is not an identity on the group $U_{\\mathbb Z}$ of upper unitriangular $m$-by-$m$ integer matrices. Every entry of any word in generic upper unitriangular matrices and their inverses is a polynomial of total degree at most $m-1$ in their upper entries. Nonidentity is preserved if each edge variable is multiplied on either side by a fixed upper unitriangular matrix.",

Lebesgue coordinate measure is both left and right Haar measure on $E$ and $\\bar E$; inversion preserves it. The Jacobian of $\\Delta_y$ on $\\bar E$ is $y^{mn_{\\mathrm f}}$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-nil-trace",
        "exact_quote": "Let $L$ be a countable group with a right action as above and let $I,J$ be finite or countable auxiliary sets. On $\\mathcal H_I=\\ell^2(L)\\otimes\\ell^2(I)$, define, for equivariant positive bounded $B$, $$\\operatorname{Tr}_I(B)\n   =\\sum_{i\\in I}\\langle B\\delta_{(1,i)},\\delta_{(1,i)}\\rangle\n       \\in[0,\\infty].$$ For a bounded equivariant $A:\\mathcal H_I\\to\\mathcal H_J$, $$\\label{nil:adjoint-square}\n \\operatorname{Tr}_I(A^*A)=\\operatorname{Tr}_J(AA^*).$$ This trace is additive on positive operators, monotone, and faithful on projections, and does not depend on the chosen orthonormal basis of the auxiliary Hilbert space. Equivariantly equivalent projections have equal size. A bounded equivariant map injective on the range of a projection $P$, with image there contained in the range of $Q$, gives $\\operatorname{Tr}(P)\\le\\operatorname{Tr}(Q)$. These statements apply to countable orthogonal sums and to each Fourier fiber. Integrating a fiber trace gives the identity trace before Fourier transformation.",
        "source_id": "PM-285.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-nil-trace",
          "exact_quote": "Let $L$ be a countable group with a right action as above and let $I,J$ be finite or countable auxiliary sets. Equivariantly equivalent projections have equal size. A bounded equivariant map injective on the range of a projection $P$, with image there contained in the range of $Q$, gives $\\operatorname{Tr}(P)\\le\\operatorname{Tr}(Q)$. These statements apply to countable orthogonal sums and to each Fourier fiber. Integrating a fiber trace gives the identity trace before Fourier transformation.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-nil-riemann",
        "exact_quote": "For positive integer weights $w_1,\\ldots,w_k$, set $$\\mathcal L_y=\\prod_{a=1}^k y^{w_a}\\mathbb Z,\\qquad\n v_y=y^{w_1+\\cdots+w_k},\\qquad 0<y\\le1.$$ For Schwartz $f$ on $\\mathbb R^k$, $$v_y\\sum_{a\\in\\mathcal L_y}f(a)\\longrightarrow\n                   \\int_{\\mathbb R^k}f(x)\\,dx.$$ The corresponding normalized sums of absolute values are uniformly bounded. For jointly Schwartz $G(a,h)$ on $\\mathbb R^k\\times\\mathbb R^k$, and every $M$, $$\\label{nil:riemann-weighted}\n \\sup_h(1+|h|)^M\n \\left|v_y\\sum_{a\\in\\mathcal L_y}G(a,h)\n                    -\\int G(a,h)\\,da\\right|\\le C_{G,M}y.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-285.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-nil-riemann",
          "exact_quote": "For positive integer weights $w_1,\\ldots,w_k$, set $$\\mathcal L_y=\\prod_{a=1}^k y^{w_a}\\mathbb Z,\\qquad\n v_y=y^{w_1+\\cdots+w_k},\\qquad 0<y\\le1.$$ For Schwartz $f$ on $\\mathbb R^k$, $$v_y\\sum_{a\\in\\mathcal L_y}f(a)\\longrightarrow\n                   \\int_{\\mathbb R^k}f(x)\\,dx.$$ The corresponding normalized sums of absolute values are uniformly bounded. For jointly Schwartz $G(a,h)$ on $\\mathbb R^k\\times\\mathbb R^k$, and every $M$, $$\\label{nil:riemann-weighted}\n \\sup_h(1+|h|)^M\n \\left|v_y\\sum_{a\\in\\mathcal L_y}G(a,h)\n                    -\\int G(a,h)\\,da\\right|\\le C_{G,M}y.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-285.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For positive integer weights $w_1,\\ldots,w_k$, set $$\\mathcal L_y=\\prod_{a=1}^k y^{w_a}\\mathbb Z,\\qquad\n v_y=y^{w_1+\\cdots+w_k},\\qquad 0<y\\le1.$$ For Schwartz $f$ on $\\mathbb R^k$, $$v_y\\sum_{a\\in\\mathcal L_y}f(a)\\longrightarrow\n                   \\int_{\\mathbb R^k}f(x)\\,dx.$$ The corresponding normalized sums of absolute values are uniformly bounded.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-nil-projection",
        "exact_quote": "There are constants $\\theta_0>0,C<\\infty$, depending only on $m$ and the two fixed Schwartz functions, with the following properties. For $0<|\\theta|<\\theta_0$, set $$\\epsilon=\\operatorname{sign}\\theta,\\quad\n y=|\\theta|^{1/m},\\quad\n \\nu=y^{mn_{\\mathrm f}}=|\\theta|^{n_{\\mathrm f}},$$ and define the norm-convergent series $$\\label{nil:K}\n K_\\theta=\\sum_{\\gamma\\in L}\n          \\nu J_\\epsilon(\\Delta_y\\gamma)u_\\theta(\\gamma).$$ It is self-adjoint and satisfies, uniformly for both signs, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|K_\\theta\\|_{\\mathrm{coeff},1}&\\le C,&\n \\|K_\\theta\\|_{\\mathrm{coeff},2}&\\asymp\\sqrt{\\nu},\n                                               \\label{nil:Knorms}\\\\\n \\|K_\\theta^2-K_\\theta\\|_{\\mathrm{coeff},1}&\\le Cy,&\n \\|K_\\theta^2-K_\\theta\\|_{\\mathrm{coeff},2}&\\le Cy\\sqrt{\\nu}.\n                                               \\label{nil:defects}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Continuous functional calculus in $C_r^*(L,\\theta c)$ gives a projection $p_\\theta$ with $$\\label{nil:projection-bounds}\n \\|p_\\theta-K_\\theta\\|\\le Cy,\\qquad\n 0<\\operatorname{tr}_\\theta(p_\\theta)\\le C|\\theta|^{n_{\\mathrm f}}.$$ These fields are measurable on the intervals $0<|\\theta|<\\theta_0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-285.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-nil-projection",
          "exact_quote": "There are constants $\\theta_0>0,C<\\infty$, depending only on $m$ and the two fixed Schwartz functions, with the following properties.         "anchor": "stmt-nil-templates",
        "exact_quote": "The functions $J_\\epsilon$ in Equation <a href=\"#nil:K\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"nil:K\">[nil:K]</a> may be replaced by smooth compactly supported $J_\\epsilon^\\flat$ at arbitrarily small uniform coefficient $\\ell^1$ cost for sufficiently small $|\\theta|$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-nil-absorption",
        "exact_quote": "On the second quotient factor, define $$V_t(\\gamma)=u_t(\\gamma)\\otimes R_\\gamma.$$ Its multiplier is $\\mathrm e((t-\\delta)c)$, and it is unitarily equivalent to $N$ copies of $u_{t-\\delta}$. The equivalence fixes the quotient-identity fiber, preserving the identity trace with its unnormalized matrix sum. Thus every coefficient sum, and every continuous spectral function of it, has the same operator norm as its evaluation in $u_{t-\\delta}$; the identity trace of a projection is multiplied by $N$.",
        "source_id": "PM-285.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-nil-absorption",
          "exact_quote": "On the second quotient factor, define $$V_t(\\gamma)=u_t(\\gamma)\\otimes R_\\gamma.$$ Its multiplier is $\\mathrm e((t-\\delta)c)$, and it is unitarily equivalent to $N$ copies of $u_{t-\\delta}$. The equivalence fixes the quotient-identity fiber, preserving the identity trace with its unnormalized matrix sum. Thus every coefficient sum, and every continuous spectral function of it, has the same operator norm as its evaluation in $u_{t-\\delta}$; the identity trace of a projection is multiplied by $N$.",
          "source_id": "PM-285.2"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-nil-window",
        "exact_quote": "There is a smooth nonnegative $h$, supported in $(-1,1)$ and positive on $[-0.7,0.7]$, with $\\sum_{i\\in\\mathbb Z}h(v-i)^2=1$ for every $v\\in\\mathbb R$. For $0<t<\\delta$, set $$\\label{nil:h}\n b(t)=\\kappa^{-1}\\log_2\\frac{t}{\\delta-t},\\qquad\n h_i(t)=h(b(t)-i),$$ and extend $h_i$ by zero to the rest of the circle. Each extension is smooth. The active set $J(t)=\\{i:h_i(t)\\ne0\\}$ has one or two elements, adjacent if two, and $\\sum_i h_i(t)^2=1$ on the open interval. With $w_i=q^{-1}2^{-\\kappa|i|}$, its support length and derivatives satisfy $$|\\operatorname{supp}h_i|\\le C_\\kappa w_i,\\qquad\n \\|\\partial_t^j h_i\\|_\\infty\\le C_{\\kappa,j}w_i^{-j}.$$ On an active index $i$, uniformly in $\\ell,i,t$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\log_2(t^{-1})\n &=\\log_2q+\\kappa\\max(-i,0)+O_\\kappa(1),\\label{nil:log-t}\\\\\n \\log_2((\\delta-t)^{-1})\n &=\\log_2q+\\kappa\\max(i,0)+O_\\kappa(1),\\label{nil:log-tau}\\\\\n t(\\delta-t)&\\le 2^\\kappa q^{-2}2^{-\\kappa|i|}.\n                                                  \\label{nil:product}\n\\end{aligned}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-285.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

        "exact_quote": "For $\\mathfrak w_i=2^{C_\\bullet H_i}$, define $$\\mu_\\ell=\\sup_{\\substack{0<t<\\delta\\\\i\\in J(t)}}\n                          \\mathfrak w_i\\,s_p(t).$$ Then $\\mu_\\ell\\to0$ as $\\ell\\to\\infty$. Furthermore $$\\|(1-\\Pi)E_*(t)\\|\\leq\\sqrt{\\beta_\\ell}\n \\label{spec:model-cut}$$ uniformly in $t$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-sam-realization",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed $\\eta>0$, and for all sufficiently large $\\ell$, there are voltages $z_e\\in F$ and matrices $W_e\\in M_N(\\mathbb C)$ on the positively oriented edges of $Y$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *There is a constant $C_\\eta$, independent of $\\ell$ and the layer, such that $\\|W_e\\|\\le C_\\eta$. Augmenting an original tower label by its matrix weight gives a finite positive alphabet of size $o(dD)$. The normalizing number $D_{ik}$ is determined by the original part of the label.*\n\n2.  *The voltage cover has no nonempty cyclically nonbacktracking closed walk based in layer $i$ of length at most $C_*H_i/\\log_2d$.*\n\n\n3.  *Let $T_P$, extended by zero on the deleted vertex indices, have entries from $B_k$ to $A_i$ $$\\label{sam:entries}\n     (T_P)_{ab}=\n     \\frac1{\\sqrt{dD_{ik}}}\\sum_{e:a\\to b}W_e\\rho(z_e),\n     \\qquad a\\in A_i,\\quad b\\in B_k,$$ with the adjoint entries in the opposite direction. Then $$\\label{sam:approx}\n     \\|T_P-\\Pi O\\Pi\\|\\le\\eta.$$ Both operators have layer width one. In particular, their central Fourier fields satisfy the same inequality almost everywhere, including $\\|T_P(t)\\|\\le\\eta$ outside the open window.*\n\n*The matrices $W_e$ are the actual weights of the individual edges of the voltage cover, so $T_P$ reduces on each component of that cover.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-sam-boxes",
        "exact_quote": "Let $x\\ge2$. Suppose a finite set of elements of $F$, including their inverses, has every integral coordinate bounded by $x^a$, where $a$ is fixed. For every fixed $B>0$ there is a finite coordinate box $\\mathcal B_x\\subset F$ with $$|\\mathcal B_x|\\le x^{c},\\qquad\n \\frac{|\\mathcal B_x\\mathbin\\triangle\\mathcal B_x g|}\n {|\\mathcal B_x|}\\le Cx^{-B}$$ for all the specified elements $g$. Constants $c,C$ depend on $a,B,F$, but not on the number of specified elements or on $x$.*\n\n\n*Let $A$ be a self-adjoint finite matrix of right convolution sums on $\\ell^2(J)\\otimes\\ell^2(F)\\otimes\\mathbb C^n$, where $J$ is a finite index set, with these group supports. Write its coefficients as $C_{uv}(g)\\in M_n(\\mathbb C)$, and suppose $$\\max_{u\\in J}\\sum_{v\\in J}\\sum_g\\|C_{uv}(g)\\|\\le R.$$ If $P_x$ retains the deck indices in $\\mathcal B_x$ and all the other indices, then $$\\label{sam:finite-test}\n \\|P_xAP_x\\|\\le\\|A\\|\n \\le\\|P_xAP_x\\|+Cx^{-B}R.$$ The same conclusion holds for a rectangular convolution matrix after self-adjoint off-diagonal doubling, using the larger of its absolute row and column sum bounds. The constants do not depend on the matrix amplification $n$.",
        "source_id": "PM-285.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-sam-walk-count",
        "exact_quote": "Let a finite graph have maximum degree $\\Delta\\ge1$. Fix a vertex $v$ and an integer $l\\ge1$. Suppose the support of every closed walk under consideration has first Betti number at most $b$. The number of length-$2l$ closed walks from $v$ in which no undirected edge occurs exactly once is at most $$C_b^l\\Delta^l.$$ Multiple edges, if present, are treated as distinct edges.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-spec-compression",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A=A^*$ on a Hilbert space, with $\\|A\\|\\leq2$, and let $E$ be a reducing projection such that $$\\|(A-I)E\\|\\leq a,\\qquad\n A|_{(1-E)\\mathcal H}\\leq(1-b)I,\\qquad 0<b<1.$$ Let $J$ be an orthogonal projection with $\\varepsilon:=\\|(1-J)E\\|\\leq1/2$. The operator $JE$ has closed range. Writing $E^J$ for the projection onto that range, put, on $J\\mathcal H$, $$B_J=E^J+(J-E^J)A(J-E^J).\n \\label{spec:compression-model}$$ Then $$\\|JAJ-B_J\\|\\leq a+20\\varepsilon,\n \\qquad B_J|_{(J-E^J)\\mathcal H}\\leq(1-b)I,\n \\qquad \\|B_J\\|\\leq2.\n \\label{spec:compression-error}$$ There is a partial isometry $V$ with $V^*V=E$, $VV^*=E^J$, and $\\|V-E\\|\\leq2\\varepsilon$. If $A,E,J$ commute with a specified unitary or projective unitary action, then so do all the projections and maps just constructed. In particular, in a setting with the identity trace of Lemma <a href=\"#nil:trace\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"nil:trace\">32</a>, $E$ and $E^J$ have equal identity traces. These statements include $E=0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-285.2"
      },

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-spec-compression",

The operator $JE$ has closed range. Writing $E^J$ for the projection onto that range, put, on $J\\mathcal H$, $$B_J=E^J+(J-E^J)A(J-E^J).\n \\label{spec:compression-model}$$ Then $$\\|JAJ-B_J\\|\\leq a+20\\varepsilon,\n \\qquad B_J|_{(J-E^J)\\mathcal H}\\leq(1-b)I,\n \\qquad \\|B_J\\|\\leq2.\n \\label{spec:compression-error}$$ There is a partial isometry $V$ with $V^*V=E$, $VV^*=E^J$, and $\\|V-E\\|\\leq2\\varepsilon$. If $A,E,J$ commute with a specified unitary or projective unitary action, then so do all the projections and maps just constructed. In particular, in a setting with the identity trace of Lemma <a href=\"#nil:trace\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"nil:trace\">32</a>, $E$ and $E^J$ have equal identity traces.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-spec-perturbation",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $B=B^*$ has a reducing projection $F$ with $BF=F$, and its complementary spectrum is contained in $[-2,1-b]$, where $0<b<1$. Let $r_0>0$ satisfy $r_0\\leq b/2$ and $r_0<1$. If $A=A^*$ and $\\|A-B\\|\\leq u<r_0$, the circle $\\mathcal C_{r_0}=\\{z:|z-1|=r_0\\}$ lies in the resolvent sets of both operators. The enclosed spectral projection $F_A$ satisfies $$\\|F_A-F\\|\\leq\\frac{u}{r_0-u}.\n \\label{spec:projection-distance}$$ Its enclosed spectrum lies in $[1-u,1+u]$; all the other spectrum of $A$ is at most $1-b+u$. If $u<r_0/2$, the projections $F_A$ and $F$ are equivalent by a partial isometry belonging to the algebra of bounded operators generated by them. This equivalence preserves any common equivariance and its identity trace.",
        "source_id": "PM-285.2"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-spec-cover-projection",
        "exact_quote": "The operator $T_P$ has a separated upper spectral projection $E_P$, obtained by integration around $\\mathcal C_{r_0}$.         "exact_quote": "For the fixed choices made above and some sufficiently large integer $\\ell$, the construction defines a finitely generated torsion-free group $\\Gamma_{\\mathrm{proj}}$, a positive integer $N$, and the self-adjoint finite group-algebra matrix $T$ in Equation <a href=\"#spec:Cayley-operator\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"spec:Cayley-operator\">[spec:Cayley-operator]</a>. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **canonical trace** ([source](#sec:scalar)): The canonical trace is $\tau_\Lambda(x)=\langle x\delta_1,\delta_1\rangle$.
- **unnormalized matrix trace** ([source](#sec:scalar)): On matrices we always use the *unnormalized* extension $$\tau_{\Lambda,N}(x)=\sum_{j=1}^N\tau_\Lambda(x_{jj}). \label{scalar:matrix-trace}$$
- **separated spectral subset** ([source](#sec:setup)): $$* </div> Here and below a spectral subset is *separated* if its distance from the rest of the spectrum is positive.
- **folded labeled graph** ([source](#pm-h-8)): A labeled graph is *folded* if, at each vertex, a prescribed oriented label occurs on at most one outgoing edge.
- **nonbacktracking path** ([source](#pm-h-8)): A path is nonbacktracking when it never immediately reverses an edge; a closed path is cyclically nonbacktracking if this also holds across its chosen basepoint.
- **formal Cayley edges** ([source](#pm-h-8)): For a labeled group presentation we retain the formal Cayley edges: a positive generator and its initial vertex specify an edge even if different formal generators have the same group value.
- **normalized biregular incidence** ([source](#pm-h-8)): A biregular incidence from a $B$-side of degree $D'$ to an $A$-side of degree $d$ is normalized by $(dD')^{-1/2}$.
- **padded bit stream** ([source](#tow:section)): Throughout, a bit stream means an element of $\mathbb F _2^{\mathbb N_0}$ with a specified finite set of active coordinates; every inactive coordinate is zero.
- **path radius** ([source](#prune:section)): Its radius at a specified position is the smaller of the numbers of edges available in the two directions from that position.
- **forest** ([source](#geo:conventions)): A forest has no nonempty finite circuit, with a loop and a pair of parallel edges counted as circuits of lengths one and two.
- **cycle space** ([source](#geo:conventions)): The cycle space is $Z_1(-;k)=\ker\partial$.
- **assigned copy domains** ([source](#stmt-geo-forest)): Put $$D_Q=V(Q)\setminus\bigcup_{R<Q}V(R),\qquad E_{\mathrm{int}}=\bigcup_Q \{e\in E(Q):\text{ both endpoints of }e\text{ lie in }D_Q\}.$$
- **weighted cover identity size** ([source](#pm-h-50)): For a surviving base vertex $v$, let $$e_v=\sum_{j=1}^N \big\langle E_P(\delta_{(v,1_F)}\otimes e_j), \delta_{(v,1_F)}\otimes e_j\big\rangle\geq0, \qquad \mathcal S=\sum_{i\in\mathbb Z}\mathfrak w_i \sum_{v\in V(Y)\cap(A_i\cup B_i)}e_v. \label{spec:weighted-size}$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-growing-degree | d | = | $2^{4\ell}$ | The single growing integer parameter determines the degree. |
| q-biregular-large-degree | D | = | $d^2$ | Determined by the base degree. |
| q-height-logarithm-bound | H_0 | ≤ | $h_*\log_2d$ | The fixed constant bounds the height uniformly in the growing parameter. |
| q-recovery-length-scale | L_i | = | $\frac{100H_i}{\log_2d}$ | Defined at every integer layer. |
| q-tower-girth-scale | g_i | = | $\frac{H_i}{100\log_2d}$ | Defined at every integer layer. |
| q-mean-centered-incidence-ceiling | Mean incidence norm on centered functions | < | $0.8$ | Uniform over full-block count, terminal active length and offsets. |
| q-cut-failure-probability | Probability of a cut below the stated volume threshold | ≤ | $\exp(-v(Z)/400)$ | The bound depends on degree volume. |
| q-realized-incidence-gap | \chi | = | $\frac1{800}$ | An absolute contraction gap before any later decrease of the gap parameter. |
| q-constant-space-pruning-error | \\|(1-\Pi)\|_C\\| | ≤ | $\sqrt{\varepsilon_\ell}$ | The deletion budget is uniform over side and layer. |
| q-coned-triangle-thinness | D_0 | = | $10^6$ | An admissible universal vertex thinness bound. |
| q-metric-graph-rank | b_1(S) | ≤ | $B(C)$ | The rank bound depends only on total length relative to girth. |
| q-unitriangular-word-degree | Total degree of a generic word entry | ≤ | $m-1$ | Independent of word length at fixed matrix size. |
| q-magnetic-matrix-dimension | N | = | $q^{n_{\mathrm f}}$ | The dimension grows with the prescribed congruence modulus and is retained without trace normalization. |
| q-empirical-list-length | M | = | $\lfloor d^{1/32}\rfloor$ | The list length depends on the tower degree. |
| q-upper-space-compression-error | \\|JAJ-B_J\\| | ≤ | $a+20\varepsilon$ | The error uses the original upper-band deviation and deletion norm. |
| q-contour-projection-distance | \\|F_A-F\\| | ≤ | $\frac{u}{r_0-u}$ | The contour radius and perturbation obey the stated strict inequalities. |
| q-weighted-localization-cost | \mathcal S | ≤ | $96\mu_\ell$ | The model weighted trace vanishes uniformly as the final parameter grows. |
| q-matrix-weighted-forest-norm | Norm of the forest adjacency | ≤ | $2w_1\sqrt{m_1}$ | Only the degree ceiling and edge-weight norm ceiling enter. |
| q-final-unnormalized-trace-ceiling | \tau_{\Gamma_{\mathrm{proj}},N}(p_0) | < | $1/2$ | The nonzero upper projection is compared equivariantly with all local upper spaces. |
