<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-subgroup-reduced-norm** [claim](#stmt-scalar-subgroup) / [proof](#proof-stmt-scalar-subgroup): Decompose the restricted regular representation into orbitwise regular representations. -> Isometric trace-preserving reduced inclusion.
- **step-free-row-compression** [claim](#stmt-scalar-compression) / [proof](#proof-stmt-scalar-compression): Separate cancelling and noncancelling free-word inputs, bound the row below and normalize its polar part. -> A scalar projection of equal trace in the free product.
- **step-coefficient-pools** [claim](#stmt-tow-coefficient-choice) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tow-coefficient-choice): Concentration and a finite union bound select injections and spills with controlled functional multiplicities. -> Full-block and prefix multiplicity bounds.
- **step-cluster-randomness** [claim](#stmt-tow-matching-clusters) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tow-matching-clusters): Triangularly parametrize slot incidences by preceding data and terminal bits. -> Independent uniform cluster matchings.
- **step-fourier-variance** [claim](#stmt-exp-fourier) / [proof](#proof-stmt-exp-fourier): Expand in finite Fourier characters and group coincident induced functionals. -> A controlled conditional mean and variance.
- **step-mean-incidence-gap** [claim](#stmt-exp-mean-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-exp-mean-gap): Use causal prefix filtrations, orthogonal difference bands and separate terminal-cutoff estimates. -> A height-uniform centered mean-incidence contraction.
- **step-cut-concentration** [claim](#stmt-exp-cut-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-exp-cut-tail): Apply hypergeometric exponential moments to internal matched edges. -> An exponential lower-tail estimate for every eligible cut.
- **step-realized-expansion** [claim](#stmt-exp-realized-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-exp-realized-gap): Avoid connected bad cuts through the local lemma, then apply the weighted Cheeger argument. -> Successful current tables and a uniform normalized incidence gap.
- **step-prefix-inversion** [claim](#stmt-prune-prefix-recovery) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prune-prefix-recovery): Alternate block recovery across the bipartition using distinct coefficients and known spills. -> Equal path words recover matching padded prefixes.
- **step-short-cycle-count** [claim](#stmt-prune-cycle-count) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prune-cycle-count): Repeat closed paths to obtain arbitrary recovery radius, then count their words. -> A uniformly small short-cycle support fraction.
- **step-prune-and-separate-heights** [claim](#stmt-prune-tower-properties) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prune-tower-properties): Delete closed gates, zero-padding ambiguities and short-cycle bases; recover a common midpoint to prove global path uniqueness. -> The common survivor with long-word uniqueness and controlled deletion.
- **step-constant-deletion-control** [claim](#stmt-prune-constant-compression) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prune-constant-compression): Sum removed squared masses on orthogonal normalized layer constants. -> A tensor-uniform constant-space compression estimate.
- **step-minimal-diagram-rules** [claim](#stmt-geo-reductions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-reductions): Minimize faces then edges and splice genuine source walks only after deck alignment. -> Reduction rules valid before and after injectivity.
- **step-short-arcs-and-euler** [claim](#stmt-geo-arcs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-arcs): Combine long-word recovery, cover girth and the plane Euler identity. -> Short arcs, many face incidences and a boundary-controlled arc count.
- **step-source-embeddings** [claim](#stmt-geo-embeddings) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-embeddings): Apply the diagram inequalities to a minimal injectivity failure and use foldedness for edges. -> Embedded component label maps and well-defined indexed copies.
- **step-coned-chain-fillings** [claim](#stmt-geo-chain-filling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-chain-filling): Replace diagram arcs by generator or spoke paths and telescope cone spokes. -> A linear short-loop decomposition of integral loop chains.
- **step-coned-thin-triangles** [claim](#stmt-geo-thin) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-thin): Construct an avoiding loop near a thick triangle point and test its chain using a Lipschitz edge form. -> Uniform vertex thinness without local finiteness.
- **step-center-avoidance** [claim](#stmt-geo-avoidance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-avoidance): Close an avoiding path through the designated center and bound its adjustable-side arcs. -> A local intrinsic distance bound for bounded avoiding paths.
- **step-one-overlap-ball** [claim](#stmt-geo-balls) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-balls): Order centers from a common root, use thin triangles to avoid a center and apply intrinsic avoidance bounds. -> One controlled tree ball containing every earlier overlap.
- **step-cycle-directness** [claim](#stmt-geo-cycle-directness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-cycle-directness): Use relator diagrams for surjectivity and the latest participating copy for injectivity. -> An algebraic direct decomposition of ambient finite-support cycles.
- **step-residual-forest** [claim](#stmt-geo-forest) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-forest): Assign vertices to earliest copies and rule out a residual circuit through its latest cycle coordinate. -> Disjoint domains and the remaining formal forest.
- **step-incidence-count** [claim](#stmt-geo-incidences) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-incidences): Normalize a source lift by deck alignment and use uniqueness of the label map at that lift. -> An equivariant bijection between copy incidences and group vertices with base types.
- **step-stabilizer-injection** [claim](#stmt-geo-stabilizers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-stabilizers): Associate a stabilizing translation with its unique deck alignment. -> Torsion-free copy stabilizers.
- **step-exact-cone-chains** [claim](#stmt-geo-acyclic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-acyclic): Eliminate spoke terms using cone triangles and use cycle directness to prove injectivity. -> An exact augmented cellular complex over every field.
- **step-exclude-torsion** [claim](#stmt-geo-torsion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-torsion): Compare the finite-length free cellular resolution with a finite cyclic group's periodic resolution. -> Finite generation and torsion-freeness of the presented group.
- **step-geometric-package** [claim](#stmt-geo-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geo-torsion): Combine embeddings, overlap balls, domain assignment, residual forest and torsion exclusion. -> The complete geometric conclusion with fixed absolute constants.
- **step-metric-rank-bound** [claim](#stmt-nil-rank) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nil-rank): Choose a separated net and map a bounded-size auxiliary graph onto fundamental groups. -> A rank bound depending only on the length-to-girth ratio.
- **step-unitriangular-word-detector** [claim](#stmt-nil-word) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nil-word): Choose long pivot arcs and use a finite matrix truncation of the Magnus expansion. -> A bounded nontrivial pivot word and a polynomial matrix detector.
- **step-graded-affine-deck-coordinates** [claim](#stmt-nil-grading) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nil-grading): Check integral polynomial laws, weight homogeneity, triangular Jacobians and first nonzero weights. -> Coordinate Haar measure and nilpotent torsion-free integer groups.
- **step-equivariant-trace-calculus** [claim](#stmt-nil-trace) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nil-trace): Reindex nonnegative matrix sums by right translation, then apply equivariant polar decomposition. -> Trace equality, comparison, faithfulness and Fourier integration.
- **step-continuous-schwartz-idempotents** [claim](#stmt-nil-schwartz) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nil-schwartz): Identify Schwartz coefficients with Schwartz kernels and pull back a Gaussian rank-one projection. -> Self-adjoint twisted convolution idempotents.
- **step-weighted-lattice-sums** [claim](#stmt-nil-riemann) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nil-riemann): Compare values over rectangular cells using derivative decay. -> Uniform weighted Riemann-sum bounds.
- **step-discrete-twisted-projections** [claim](#stmt-nil-projection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nil-projection): Control sampled coefficient defects in both norms and separate the resulting spectral intervals by functional calculus. -> Nonzero measurable discrete projections with small identity trace.
- **step-compact-coefficient-templates** [claim](#stmt-nil-templates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nil-templates): Cut off Schwartz tails and control relative dilations through the mean value theorem. -> Compact coefficient templates stable under logarithmic scale rounding.
- **step-magnetic-absorption** [claim](#stmt-nil-absorption) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nil-absorption): Conjugate by a diagonal unitary built from the finite magnetic representation. -> The shifted regular twist with exact unnormalized multiplicity.
- **step-moving-windows** [claim](#stmt-nil-window) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nil-window): Normalize translates of a smooth bump and pull back through the logarithmic endpoint ratio. -> Adjacent active layers and endpoint-dependent derivative and support bounds.
- **step-weighted-product-trace** [claim](#stmt-nil-weighted) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nil-weighted): Combine the two endpoint trace factors with the window product estimate and fixed parameter inequalities. -> Uniformly vanishing weighted fiber trace.
- **step-model-band** [claim](#stmt-spec-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-model): Separate layer constants from centered functions and identify the symmetric upper space. -> A model upper band with exact trace and controlled pruning loss.
- **step-finite-regular-norm-tests** [claim](#stmt-sam-boxes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sam-boxes): Build graded nearly invariant boxes and average compression quadratic forms over left translates. -> Finite norm tests with controlled regular-norm error.
- **step-empirical-matrix-lists** [claim](#stmt-sam-empirical) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sam-empirical): Compress to a finite norm test and expand centered even moments, eliminating singleton sample indices. -> Simultaneously accurate finite empirical lists.
- **step-closed-walk-moment-count** [claim](#stmt-sam-walk-count) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sam-walk-count): Encode core edges by pushes and pops, then encode attached tree excursions. -> A degree-scaled exponential bound on eligible closed walks.
- **step-simultaneous-edge-realization** [claim](#stmt-sam-realization) / [proof](#pm-h-47): Use compact templates and empirical lists, polynomial holonomy tests, centered block moments and countable compactness. -> One voltage and matrix-weight assignment with full regular approximation and cover girth.
- **step-compressed-upper-space** [claim](#stmt-spec-compression) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-compression): Normalize the compressed projection by polar decomposition and estimate its upper and off-diagonal blocks. -> A nearby comparison operator and an equivalent compressed upper projection.
- **step-contour-stability** [claim](#stmt-spec-perturbation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-perturbation): Use a resolvent Neumann series, contour integration and close-projection polar normalization. -> Controlled projection distance and equivariant equivalence.
- **step-actual-cover-upper-projection** [claim](#stmt-spec-cover-projection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-cover-projection): Apply compression and contour stability fiberwise with a common separating contour. -> A nonzero cover projection with exact fiber trace and component reduction.
- **step-layer-trace-localization** [claim](#stmt-spec-localization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-localization): Expand the resolvent by layer width, discard contour terms unable to reach active layers and sum weighted trace tails. -> Small weighted identity size without additional cardinality factors.
- **step-trim-local-copies** [claim](#stmt-spec-copy-cut) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-copy-cut): Bound the finite-ball Hilbert–Schmidt loss using base diagonals and apply upper-space compression. -> Local domain comparison operators with equivalent upper spaces.
- **step-weighted-forest-norm** [claim](#stmt-spec-forest-norm) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-forest-norm): Root every component and split adjacency into parent-to-child and adjoint contributions. -> A square-root-degree adjacency bound.
- **step-global-cayley-band** [claim](#stmt-spec-global-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-global-gap): Compare the Cayley operator with disjoint compressed copies and bound residual forest edges before contour perturbation. -> A nonempty separated reduced spectral band and its reduced-algebra projection.
- **step-all-copy-overlap-loss** [claim](#stmt-spec-lost-restrictions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-lost-restrictions): Apply finite-ball Cauchy–Schwarz and sum nonnegative terms through the exact incidence count. -> A uniform bound on the sum of lost local upper restrictions.
- **step-equivariant-analysis-map** [claim](#stmt-spec-restriction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-restriction): Combine disjoint retained restrictions with the overlap-loss bound, then compare polar projections equivariantly. -> A bounded equivariant restriction map with a small kernel complement.
- **step-global-trace-injection** [claim](#stmt-spec-kernel-form) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-kernel-form): Bound the quadratic form on the restriction kernel and separate it from the global upper spectrum. -> An equivariant injection yielding a strictly positive small unnormalized trace.
- **step-final-matrix-construction** [claim](#stmt-spec-matrix-projection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-matrix-projection): Respect the fixed dependency order and increase the single final parameter to meet every uniform tolerance. -> The final finite group-algebra matrix and its reduced projection.
- **step-matrix-target-completion** [claim](#stmt-setup-matrix-target) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-matrix-projection-2): Read the final matrix construction in the original reduced regular convention. -> The announced matrix target.
- **step-scalar-counterexample-completion** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-matrix-projection-2): Adjoin free generators and apply scalar compression to the small-trace matrix projection. -> A nontrivial scalar projection over a finitely generated torsion-free group.

          "anchor": "stmt-geo-arcs",
          "exact_quote": "Every arc incident with a bounded face has length less than $L_i$, when read from an endpoint in layer $i$ on that face. If $b$ is the number of arcs and there are at most $m$ marked vertices, then $$b\\le 3J+3m-3,\\qquad 20J\\le 2b,\\qquad b\\le 6m.\n \\label{geo:euler}$$ These statements require only $C_*\\ge10^4$ and sufficiently large $\\ell$.",
          "source_id": "PM-285.2"
        }
      ],

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-geo-main",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-geo-main",
          "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $C_*,C_\\bullet$ such that, for sufficiently large $\\ell$, the preceding hypotheses have the following consequences. Every component map $\\phi:C\\to X$ is an embedding. Index copies by pairs $(C,\\phi)$ modulo the equivalence $$(C,\\phi)\\sim\n (\\delta_a C,\\phi\\delta_a^{-1}),\\qquad a\\in F.$$ Thus an indexed copy $Q$ remembers its source component and source vertices up to this deck alignment, in addition to its embedded image. There is a well-order of these copies and an induced intrinsic ball $B(Q)$ in each $Q$ such that*\n\n1.  *every vertex of $Q$ shared with an earlier copy belongs to $B(Q)$;*\n\n2.  *the subgraph of $Q$ consisting of all edges with at least one endpoint in $B(Q)$ is a forest;*\n\n3.  *for every vertex of $B(Q)$ lying over layer $i$, $$|V(B(Q))|\\le\\mathfrak w_i,\n     \\qquad \\mathfrak w_i=2^{C_\\bullet H_i};\n     \\label{geo:weight}$$*\n\n4.  *if $D_Q$ is $V(Q)$ minus its vertices shared with earlier copies, the domains $D_Q$ are pairwise disjoint, and deleting every edge of $Q$ whose two endpoints lie in $D_Q$, for all $Q$, leaves a forest in $X$.*\n\n*Furthermore $\\Gamma_{\\mathrm{proj}}$ is torsion-free. None of the constants depends on $F$, the voltage values, the number of components of $P$, or $|S|$.",
          "source_id": "PM-285.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-geometric-package",
      "inputs": [
        "Cover girth and recovery hypotheses"

      "method": "Combine embeddings, overlap balls, domain assignment, residual forest and torsion exclusion.",
      "output": "The complete geometric conclusion with fixed absolute constants.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-geo-torsion"
    },

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-nil-rank",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-nil-rank",
          "exact_quote": "Let $S$ be a finite connected metric graph with positive edge lengths, total length at most $Cg$, and girth greater than $g>0$. Girth means the infimum of lengths of embedded circles, with value $+\\infty$ for a tree. Put $$B(C)=\\max\\{1,\\lceil20C\\rceil\\}^{\\,2}.$$ Then $b_1(S)\\le B(C)$. Vertices of degree one and parallel edges are allowed.",
          "source_id": "PM-285.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-metric-rank-bound",
      "inputs": [
        "Finite metric graph",

      "method": "Choose a separated net and map a bounded-size auxiliary graph onto fundamental groups.",

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-nil-word",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-metric-rank-bound"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-nil-word",
          "exact_quote": "Let $W$ be a nonempty cyclically nonbacktracking closed walk in a unit-edge graph of girth greater than $g$, with length at most $Cg$. There is a spanning tree of its support such that, after its edge variables are set equal to the identity, the holonomy word has a nontrivial free reduction of length at most $$L(C)=\\lceil3CB(C)\\rceil.$$ If $m>L(C)$, that word is not an identity on the group $U_{\\mathbb Z}$ of upper unitriangular $m$-by-$m$ integer matrices. Every entry of any word in generic upper unitriangular matrices and their inverses is a polynomial of total degree at most $m-1$ in their upper entries. Nonidentity is preserved if each edge variable is multiplied on either side by a fixed upper unitriangular matrix.",
          "source_id": "PM-285.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-unitriangular-word-detector",
      "inputs": [
        "Metric rank bound",
        "Cyclically nonbacktracking walk"

      "method": "Choose long pivot arcs and use a finite matrix truncation of the Magnus expansion.",

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-nil-word"

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-nil-grading",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-nil-grading",
          "exact_quote": "The laws above are polynomial with integer coefficients in $(\\lambda,b-1,z)$. Give the coordinates weights $$\\operatorname{wt}(b-1)_{ij}=j-i,\\quad\n \\operatorname{wt}\\lambda_{ij}=m-(j-i),\\quad\n \\operatorname{wt}z=m.$$ Scaling weight $a$ by $y^a$ defines an automorphism $\\Delta_y$, for $y>0$, and $c(\\Delta_y\\gamma,\\Delta_y\\eta)=y^m c(\\gamma,\\eta)$. Lebesgue coordinate measure is both left and right Haar measure on $E$ and $\\bar E$; inversion preserves it. The Jacobian of $\\Delta_y$ on $\\bar E$ is $y^{mn_{\\mathrm f}}$. The integer coordinate sets $E_{\\mathbb Z}$ and $\\bar E_{\\mathbb Z}$ are countable nilpotent torsion-free groups.",
          "source_id": "PM-285.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-graded-affine-deck-coordinates",
      "inputs": [
        "Affine group law",
        "Positive coordinate weights"

      "method": "Check integral polynomial laws, weight homogeneity, triangular Jacobians and first nonzero weights.",
      "output": "Coordinate Haar measure and nilpotent torsion-free integer groups.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-nil-grading"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-nil-trace",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-nil-trace",
          "exact_quote": "Let $L$ be a countable group with a right action as above and let $I,J$ be finite or countable auxiliary sets. On $\\mathcal H_I=\\ell^2(L)\\otimes\\ell^2(I)$, define, for equivariant positive bounded $B$, $$\\operatorname{Tr}_I(B)\n   =\\sum_{i\\in I}\\langle B\\delta_{(1,i)},\\delta_{(1,i)}\\rangle\n       \\in[0,\\infty].$$ For a bounded equivariant $A:\\mathcal H_I\\to\\mathcal H_J$, $$\\label{nil:adjoint-square}\n \\operatorname{Tr}_I(A^*A)=\\operatorname{Tr}_J(AA^*).$$ This trace is additive on positive operators, monotone, and faithful on projections, and does not depend on the chosen orthonormal basis of the auxiliary Hilbert space. Equivariantly equivalent projections have equal size. A bounded equivariant map injective on the range of a projection $P$, with image there contained in the range of $Q$, gives $\\operatorname{Tr}(P)\\le\\operatorname{Tr}(Q)$. These statements apply to countable orthogonal sums and to each Fourier fiber. Integrating a fiber trace gives the identity trace before Fourier transformation.",
          "source_id": "PM-285.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-equivariant-trace-calculus",
      "inputs": [

      "method": "Reindex nonnegative matrix sums by right translation, then apply equivariant polar decomposition.",
      "output": "Trace equality, comparison, faithfulness and Fourier integration.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-nil-trace"

      "inputs": [

          "exact_quote": "For positive integer weights $w_1,\\ldots,w_k$, set $$\\mathcal L_y=\\prod_{a=1}^k y^{w_a}\\mathbb Z,\\qquad\n v_y=y^{w_1+\\cdots+w_k},\\qquad 0<y\\le1.$$ For Schwartz $f$ on $\\mathbb R^k$, $$v_y\\sum_{a\\in\\mathcal L_y}f(a)\\longrightarrow\n                   \\int_{\\mathbb R^k}f(x)\\,dx.$$ The corresponding normalized sums of absolute values are uniformly bounded. For jointly Schwartz $G(a,h)$ on $\\mathbb R^k\\times\\mathbb R^k$, and every $M$, $$\\label{nil:riemann-weighted}\n \\sup_h(1+|h|)^M\n \\left|v_y\\sum_{a\\in\\mathcal L_y}G(a,h)\n                    -\\int G(a,h)\\,da\\right|\\le C_{G,M}y.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-285.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-weighted-lattice-sums",
      "inputs": [
        "Positive integer coordinate weights",
        "Schwartz functions"

      "method": "Compare values over rectangular cells using derivative decay.",

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-nil-riemann"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-nil-projection",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-continuous-schwartz-idempotents",
        "step-weighted-lattice-sums",
        "step-equivariant-trace-calculus"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-nil-projection",
          "exact_quote": "There are constants $\\theta_0>0,C<\\infty$, depending only on $m$ and the two fixed Schwartz functions, with the following properties. For $0<|\\theta|<\\theta_0$, set $$\\epsilon=\\operatorname{sign}\\theta,\\quad\n y=|\\theta|^{1/m},\\quad\n \\nu=y^{mn_{\\mathrm f}}=|\\theta|^{n_{\\mathrm f}},$$ and define the norm-convergent series $$\\label{nil:K}\n K_\\theta=\\sum_{\\gamma\\in L}\n          \\nu J_\\epsilon(\\Delta_y\\gamma)u_\\theta(\\gamma).$$ It is self-adjoint and satisfies, uniformly for both signs, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|K_\\theta\\|_{\\mathrm{coeff},1}&\\le C,&\n \\|K_\\theta\\|_{\\mathrm{coeff},2}&\\asymp\\sqrt{\\nu},\n                                               \\label{nil:Knorms}\\\\\n \\|K_\\theta^2-K_\\theta\\|_{\\mathrm{coeff},1}&\\le Cy,&\n \\|K_\\theta^2-K_\\theta\\|_{\\mathrm{coeff},2}&\\le Cy\\sqrt{\\nu}.\n                                               \\label{nil:defects}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Continuous functional calculus in $C_r^*(L,\\theta c)$ gives a projection $p_\\theta$ with $$\\label{nil:projection-bounds}\n \\|p_\\theta-K_\\theta\\|\\le Cy,\\qquad\n 0<\\operatorname{tr}_\\theta(p_\\theta)\\le C|\\theta|^{n_{\\mathrm f}}.$$ These fields are measurable on the intervals $0<|\\theta|<\\theta_0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-285.2"
        }
      ],

For fixed such a function, replacing $\\alpha>0$ by $\\beta>0$, where $|\\log(\\alpha/\\beta)|\\le a\\le1$, changes the coefficients $$\\alpha^{n_{\\mathrm f}}\n     J_\\epsilon^\\flat(\\Delta_{\\alpha^{1/m}}\\gamma)$$ by $\\ell^1$ amount at most $Ca$, uniformly for sufficiently small $\\alpha,\\beta$.       "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-nil-absorption",
          "exact_quote": "On the second quotient factor, define $$V_t(\\gamma)=u_t(\\gamma)\\otimes R_\\gamma.$$ Its multiplier is $\\mathrm e((t-\\delta)c)$, and it is unitarily equivalent to $N$ copies of $u_{t-\\delta}$. The equivalence fixes the quotient-identity fiber, preserving the identity trace with its unnormalized matrix sum. Thus every coefficient sum, and every continuous spectral function of it, has the same operator norm as its evaluation in $u_{t-\\delta}$; the identity trace of a projection is multiplied by $N$.",

    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-nil-window",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-nil-window",
          "exact_quote": "There is a smooth nonnegative $h$, supported in $(-1,1)$ and positive on $[-0.7,0.7]$, with $\\sum_{i\\in\\mathbb Z}h(v-i)^2=1$ for every $v\\in\\mathbb R$. For $0<t<\\delta$, set $$\\label{nil:h}\n b(t)=\\kappa^{-1}\\log_2\\frac{t}{\\delta-t},\\qquad\n h_i(t)=h(b(t)-i),$$ and extend $h_i$ by zero to the rest of the circle. Each extension is smooth. The active set $J(t)=\\{i:h_i(t)\\ne0\\}$ has one or two elements, adjacent if two, and $\\sum_i h_i(t)^2=1$ on the open interval. With $w_i=q^{-1}2^{-\\kappa|i|}$, its support length and derivatives satisfy $$|\\operatorname{supp}h_i|\\le C_\\kappa w_i,\\qquad\n \\|\\partial_t^j h_i\\|_\\infty\\le C_{\\kappa,j}w_i^{-j}.$$ On an active index $i$, uniformly in $\\ell,i,t$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\log_2(t^{-1})\n &=\\log_2q+\\kappa\\max(-i,0)+O_\\kappa(1),\\label{nil:log-t}\\\\\n \\log_2((\\delta-t)^{-1})\n &=\\log_2q+\\kappa\\max(i,0)+O_\\kappa(1),\\label{nil:log-tau}\\\\\n t(\\delta-t)&\\le 2^\\kappa q^{-2}2^{-\\kappa|i|}.\n                                                  \\label{nil:product}\n\\end{aligned}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-285.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-moving-windows",
      "inputs": [
        "Fixed window scale"
      ],
      "method": "Normalize translates of a smooth bump and pull back through the logarithmic endpoint ratio.",
      "output": "Adjacent active layers and endpoint-dependent derivative and support bounds.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-nil-window"
    },

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-nil-weighted",
          "exact_quote": "For $\\mathfrak w_i=2^{C_\\bullet H_i}$, define $$\\mu_\\ell=\\sup_{\\substack{0<t<\\delta\\\\i\\in J(t)}}\n                          \\mathfrak w_i\\,s_p(t).$$ Then $\\mu_\\ell\\to0$ as $\\ell\\to\\infty$. More explicitly, $$\\label{nil:weighted-estimate}\n \\mathfrak w_i N t^{n_{\\mathrm f}}(\\delta-t)^{n_{\\mathrm f}}\n \\le C d^{-(A_*n_{\\mathrm f}-C_\\bullet h_*)}\n          2^{-(\\kappa n_{\\mathrm f}-C_\\bullet)|i|}\n                  \\qquad(i\\in J(t)).$$ The two exponents subtracted on the right are positive.",
          "source_id": "PM-285.2"
        }

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-nil-weighted"
    },

      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-spec-model",
          "exact_quote": "For $0<t<\\delta$, define an isometry on $p(t)\\mathcal H_t$ by $$V_*(t)\\zeta=2^{-1/2}\n \\left(\\bigoplus_i h_i(t)a_i\\otimes\\zeta\\right)\n \\oplus\n 2^{-1/2}\\left(\\bigoplus_i h_i(t)b_i\\otimes\\zeta\\right),\n \\qquad E_*(t)=V_*(t)V_*(t)^*.\n \\label{spec:model-isometry}$$ Then $O(t)E_*(t)=E_*(t)$, $\\|O(t)\\|\\leq1$, and $$O(t)|_{(1-E_*(t))\\mathcal H^0_t}\\leq(1-\\chi)I,\n \\qquad\n \\operatorname{tr}_{\\mathrm{id}}E_*(t)\n       =\\operatorname{tr}_{\\mathrm{id}}p(t).\n \\label{spec:model-gap-trace}$$ Set $E_*(t)=0$ outside the window; the same upper bound holds there. Furthermore $$\\|(1-\\Pi)E_*(t)\\|\\leq\\sqrt{\\beta_\\ell}\n \\label{spec:model-cut}$$ uniformly in $t$.",
          "source_id": "PM-285.2"

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-sam-boxes",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-sam-boxes",
          "exact_quote": "Let $x\\ge2$. Suppose a finite set of elements of $F$, including their inverses, has every integral coordinate bounded by $x^a$, where $a$ is fixed. For every fixed $B>0$ there is a finite coordinate box $\\mathcal B_x\\subset F$ with $$|\\mathcal B_x|\\le x^{c},\\qquad\n \\frac{|\\mathcal B_x\\mathbin\\triangle\\mathcal B_x g|}\n {|\\mathcal B_x|}\\le Cx^{-B}$$ for all the specified elements $g$. Constants $c,C$ depend on $a,B,F$, but not on the number of specified elements or on $x$.*\n\n\n*Let $A$ be a self-adjoint finite matrix of right convolution sums on $\\ell^2(J)\\otimes\\ell^2(F)\\otimes\\mathbb C^n$, where $J$ is a finite index set, with these group supports. Write its coefficients as $C_{uv}(g)\\in M_n(\\mathbb C)$, and suppose $$\\max_{u\\in J}\\sum_{v\\in J}\\sum_g\\|C_{uv}(g)\\|\\le R.$$ If $P_x$ retains the deck indices in $\\mathcal B_x$ and all the other indices, then $$\\label{sam:finite-test}\n \\|P_xAP_x\\|\\le\\|A\\|\n \\le\\|P_xAP_x\\|+Cx^{-B}R.$$ The same conclusion holds for a rectangular convolution matrix after self-adjoint off-diagonal doubling, using the larger of its absolute row and column sum bounds. The constants do not depend on the matrix amplification $n$.",
          "source_id": "PM-285.2"
        }
      ],

Suppose $\\|W\\|\\le B_0$, every possible group coordinate is bounded by $q^a$, and $N\\le q^a$, for fixed constants $a,B_0,C$. For each fixed $\\varepsilon>0$, and sufficiently large $\\ell$, each expectation has an $M$-term empirical average within $\\varepsilon$ in the full regular norm. The averages may be chosen for all the distributions simultaneously.",

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-sam-realization",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-sam-realization",
          "exact_quote": "For every fixed $\\eta>0$, and for all sufficiently large $\\ell$, there are voltages $z_e\\in F$ and matrices $W_e\\in M_N(\\mathbb C)$ on the positively oriented edges of $Y$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *There is a constant $C_\\eta$, independent of $\\ell$ and the layer, such that $\\|W_e\\|\\le C_\\eta$. Augmenting an original tower label by its matrix weight gives a finite positive alphabet of size $o(dD)$. The normalizing number $D_{ik}$ is determined by the original part of the label.*\n\n2.  *The voltage cover has no nonempty cyclically nonbacktracking closed walk based in layer $i$ of length at most $C_*H_i/\\log_2d$.*\n\n\n3.  *Let $T_P$, extended by zero on the deleted vertex indices, have entries from $B_k$ to $A_i$ $$\\label{sam:entries}\n     (T_P)_{ab}=\n     \\frac1{\\sqrt{dD_{ik}}}\\sum_{e:a\\to b}W_e\\rho(z_e),\n     \\qquad a\\in A_i,\\quad b\\in B_k,$$ with the adjoint entries in the opposite direction. Then $$\\label{sam:approx}\n     \\|T_P-\\Pi O\\Pi\\|\\le\\eta.$$ Both operators have layer width one. In particular, their central Fourier fields satisfy the same inequality almost everywhere, including $\\|T_P(t)\\|\\le\\eta$ outside the open window.*\n\n*The matrices $W_e$ are the actual weights of the individual edges of the voltage cover, so $T_P$ reduces on each component of that cover.",
          "source_id": "PM-285.2"
        }

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-spec-compression",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-spec-compression",
          "exact_quote": "Let $A=A^*$ on a Hilbert space, with $\\|A\\|\\leq2$, and let $E$ be a reducing projection such that $$\\|(A-I)E\\|\\leq a,\\qquad\n A|_{(1-E)\\mathcal H}\\leq(1-b)I,\\qquad 0<b<1.$$ Let $J$ be an orthogonal projection with $\\varepsilon:=\\|(1-J)E\\|\\leq1/2$. The operator $JE$ has closed range. Writing $E^J$ for the projection onto that range, put, on $J\\mathcal H$, $$B_J=E^J+(J-E^J)A(J-E^J).\n \\label{spec:compression-model}$$ Then $$\\|JAJ-B_J\\|\\leq a+20\\varepsilon,\n \\qquad B_J|_{(J-E^J)\\mathcal H}\\leq(1-b)I,\n \\qquad \\|B_J\\|\\leq2.\n \\label{spec:compression-error}$$ There is a partial isometry $V$ with $V^*V=E$, $VV^*=E^J$, and $\\|V-E\\|\\leq2\\varepsilon$. If $A,E,J$ commute with a specified unitary or projective unitary action, then so do all the projections and maps just constructed. In particular, in a setting with the identity trace of Lemma <a href=\"#nil:trace\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"nil:trace\">32</a>, $E$ and $E^J$ have equal identity traces. These statements include $E=0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-285.2"
        }

          "anchor": "stmt-spec-perturbation",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose $B=B^*$ has a reducing projection $F$ with $BF=F$, and its complementary spectrum is contained in $[-2,1-b]$, where $0<b<1$. Let $r_0>0$ satisfy $r_0\\leq b/2$ and $r_0<1$. If $A=A^*$ and $\\|A-B\\|\\leq u<r_0$, the circle $\\mathcal C_{r_0}=\\{z:|z-1|=r_0\\}$ lies in the resolvent sets of both operators. The enclosed spectral projection $F_A$ satisfies $$\\|F_A-F\\|\\leq\\frac{u}{r_0-u}.\n \\label{spec:projection-distance}$$ Its enclosed spectrum lies in $[1-u,1+u]$; all the other spectrum of $A$ is at most $1-b+u$. If $u<r_0/2$, the projections $F_A$ and $F$ are equivalent by a partial isometry belonging to the algebra of bounded operators generated by them. This equivalence preserves any common equivariance and its identity trace.",

Its kernel $K$ is a closed invariant subspace, and every $\\xi\\in K$ satisfies $$E_Q(\\xi|_{V(Q)})=0\\quad\\text{for every indexed }Q.\n \\label{spec:kernel-restrictions}$$ Moreover, $$\\operatorname{Tr}_{\\Gamma_{\\mathrm{proj}}} P_{K^\\perp}\\leq\\mathcal S_0.\n \\label{spec:kernel-complement-size}$$",

          "anchor": "stmt-spec-matrix-projection",
          "exact_quote": "For the fixed choices made above and some sufficiently large integer $\\ell$, the construction defines a finitely generated torsion-free group $\\Gamma_{\\mathrm{proj}}$, a positive integer $N$, and the self-adjoint finite group-algebra matrix $T$ in Equation <a href=\"#spec:Cayley-operator\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"spec:Cayley-operator\">[spec:Cayley-operator]</a>. Its spectrum in the reduced representation has the separation in Equation <a href=\"#spec:global-spectrum\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"spec:global-spectrum\">[spec:global-spectrum]</a>. The resulting projection $p_0$ in Equation <a href=\"#spec:p0\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"spec:p0\">[spec:p0]</a> belongs to $M_N(C_r^*(\\Gamma_{\\mathrm{proj}}))$ and obeys $$0<\\sum_{j=1}^N\\tau_{\\Gamma_{\\mathrm{proj}}}((p_0)_{jj})<\\frac12.$$",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-small-unnormalized-trace**: The constructed matrix projection has strictly positive unnormalized canonical trace below the scalar-compression threshold. The matrix indices contribute their ordinary sum.
- **invariant-scalar-compression**: A matrix projection satisfying the small unnormalized trace hypothesis gives a scalar projection of the same trace after adjoining the prescribed free generators. Finite generation and torsion-freeness are preserved when the original group has them.
- **invariant-full-regular-approximation**: The same edge voltages and matrix weights satisfy the voltage-cover girth condition and approximate the compressed model in the full regular operator norm. Finite representation tests alone are insufficient.
- **invariant-separated-reduced-band**: The Cayley operator has a nonempty separated upper spectral band. Continuous functional calculus places its upper projection in the matrix reduced group algebra.
- **invariant-equivariant-trace-comparison**: The restriction operator over all indexed copies is bounded and equivariant. The global upper space injects equivariantly into its kernel complement, yielding the small unnormalized trace bound.
- **invariant-forest-adjacency-bound**: A self-adjoint matrix adjacency on a countable forest is bounded in norm by the stated expression involving maximum degree and maximum edge-weight norm.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-normalized-matrix-trace**: Divide the matrix trace by the matrix dimension before checking scalar compression. -> A small normalized trace does not establish the required small unnormalized trace, so the corner-invertibility argument is unsupported.
- **anti-group-ring-projection**: Treat the resulting scalar projection as a finite group-ring idempotent. -> The constructed projection belongs to the reduced completion; the paper explicitly distinguishes this from the group ring.
- **anti-kernel-dimension-certificate**: Infer reduced-algebra membership from a nonintegral von Neumann kernel dimension alone. -> The kernel projection need not lie in the reduced completion without the required spectral separation.
- **anti-finite-representation-substitution**: Replace the full regular-norm approximation by agreement in selected finite-dimensional representations. -> Those tests do not supply the operator-norm control used to transfer the separated band.
- **anti-domain-trace-equivariance**: Compute canonical trace by assuming that the well-ordered assigned domains are equivariant. -> The ordinary equivalence with the assigned-domain upper space does not establish an equivariant trace comparison.
- **anti-independent-layer-parameters**: Choose a different sufficiently large tower parameter separately for each layer. -> Layerwise choices do not establish the simultaneous uniform construction or its common spectral tolerances.
- **anti-cyclic-residual-graph**: Apply the forest adjacency bound to a residual graph whose cycles have not been excluded. -> The parent-to-child decomposition used in the norm proof depends on the residual graph being a forest.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The principal construction is not a finite executable projection construction. The source states: “The group is given by an infinite graphical presentation followed by a free product with a finitely generated free group.” It also states: “The choices of labeling tables and edge voltages are specified by finite distributions and proved simultaneous existence statements.” The simultaneous realization uses countably many edge requirements and a compactness argument, while the final projection uses continuous functional calculus in the infinite regular representation. A finite matrix dimension and finite group-algebra support for the operator do not supply a finite representation of its spectral projection. Finite checks of auxiliary graph or matrix identities would cover restricted subsidiary cases and would not certify the principal construction or its global spectral and trace conclusions.

At corresponding $A$-positions of radius at least $2j+1$, both padded streams agree through length $r+sj$, for every $j\\geq0$. At corresponding $B$-positions of radius at least $2j$, both padded streams agree through length $sj$, for every $j\\geq1$. The compared positions need not be in the same layer.",
          "source_object": "stmt-prune-prefix-recovery"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "proof-stmt-prune-prefix-recovery",
          "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-prune-prefix-recovery</summary>\n\n*Proof.* The orientation of a letter identifies the side of either endpoint. Reading its underlying positive label gives the family, the serial number and $x=b\\Vert x'$, and therefore both slots. In particular, the same word identifies the same slot names, diagonal coefficients, spill maps, and family rules at corresponding positions. It also identifies the signs and residues modulo $K$ of the layer indices, although their absolute values may differ.\n\n\nAt an interior position the two incident slots are distinct. Otherwise the incoming and outgoing edges would be the unique edge at the same slot, so the path would backtrack. Write their evaluations on a current block as $$Q^a_h=v_h+\\lambda_a u_h+S_a u_{h-1},\\qquad\n Q^b_h=v_h+\\lambda_b u_h+S_b u_{h-1}.$$ Here $u_h,v_h$ are the full padded current blocks, multiplication uses their corresponding field, and the last terms are the spills from the preceding block, extended by zero as needed. For the first block there is no preceding spill. If the two evaluation blocks and the preceding input block have been recovered, subtracting gives $$(\\lambda_a-\\lambda_b)u_h\n   =Q^a_h-Q^b_h-(S_a-S_b)u_{h-1}.\n \\label{prune:block-inversion}$$ The coefficient difference is nonzero and hence invertible. Thus $u_h$, followed by $v_h$, is recovered. Different spill lengths for the two incident slots do not affect this argument.\n\nCall the asserted $A$-statement at index $j$ the assertion $\\mathcal A_j$, and the $B$-statement $\\mathcal B_j$. Interpret $\\mathcal B_0$ as the vacuous recovery of the empty prefix. At radius one on side $A$, the first $r$ bits of each incident evaluation are present in its label $x$, because $p=r+\\alpha\\geq r$. Equation <a href=\"#prune:block-inversion\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prune:block-inversion\">[prune:block-inversion]</a> for the first block proves $\\mathcal A_0$.\n\nSuppose $j\\geq1$. At a $B$-position of radius $2j$, both neighbors satisfy $\\mathcal A_{j-1}$; centrally we already have $\\mathcal B_{j-1}$. For either incident edge, the second equation of <a href=\"#tow:edge-equations\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"tow:edge-equations\">[tow:edge-equations]</a> shows that to know $Q_B$ through length $sj$, beyond the label prefix $x'$, it suffices to know the $\\mathcal J$-output through length $$sj-p'=r+s(j-1)-\\beta\\leq r+s(j-1).$$ All source $A$-blocks involved are known in full at the neighbor. Every key for the transformation of $A$-block $j-1$, or an earlier block, uses central $B$-blocks at most $j-1$, together with earlier blocks of that neighbor’s $A$-stream. These are all known. Consequently the indicated output prefix, including every gate decision, agrees in the two paths. Both central evaluation prefixes therefore agree through $sj$. Their preceding spill inputs are already known by $\\mathcal B_{j-1}$, so <a href=\"#prune:block-inversion\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prune:block-inversion\">[prune:block-inversion]</a> recovers the next full $B$-block. This proves $\\mathcal B_j$.\n\nAt an $A$-position of radius $2j+1$, both neighbors satisfy $\\mathcal B_j$, and centrally we have $\\mathcal A_{j-1}$. By the first equation of <a href=\"#tow:edge-equations\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"tow:edge-equations\">[tow:edge-equations]</a>, the evaluation prefix through $r+sj$ needs only the $\\mathcal F$-output through $$r+sj-p=sj-\\alpha\\leq sj.$$ The required neighbor $B$-blocks through block $j$ are fully known. The keys for such a block use central $A$-blocks only through block $j-1$, and preceding neighbor $B$-blocks. These again are known, so both evaluation prefixes agree. Subtract the already known preceding spill and apply <a href=\"#prune:block-inversion\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"prune:block-inversion\">[prune:block-inversion]</a>. This proves $\\mathcal A_j$ and completes the alternating induction.\n\nThis proof uses full padded blocks. A permutation may mix all the bits of a block, but its entire source block is already known at the neighbor before it is used. Inactive bits cause no difficulty because the equations are padded-stream equations. When a rule lies beyond one of the compared paths’ actual cutoffs, it is still the same family rule on both paths; equality of the preceding input blocks gives equality of its key and gate. This is precisely why Lemma <a href=\"#prune:prefix-recovery\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prune:prefix-recovery\">11</a> is proved in $Y^\\#$, with its family-wide gated rules. The inequalities used above also include $\\alpha=0$, $\\beta=0$, and a final partial block of any length $1\\leq t\\leq s$. ◻",
          "source_object": "proof-stmt-prune-prefix-recovery"
        }
      ],
      "space_complexity": null,
      "strength": "moderate",

      "why": "The proof supplies a finite recovery procedure for stream prefixes from a nonbacktracking label word in the prescribed tower. Its use requires the actual coefficient maps, spill maps, family rules, and sufficiently large path radius."
    },

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-existential-group-scope** (counterexample_scope): The counterexample concerns the constructed finitely generated torsion-free group; it does not assert that every torsion-free group admits a nontrivial reduced-algebra projection. Blocked use: ['Apply the existence conclusion to an arbitrary torsion-free group without constructing the required spectral band.']
- **limit-completion-versus-group-ring** (formal_scope): The scalar projection belongs to the reduced completion and is explicitly distinguished from an idempotent in the complex group ring. Blocked use: ['Represent the resulting scalar projection as a finitely supported group-ring idempotent.', 'Treat the projection counterexample as a counterexample to the characteristic-zero group-ring idempotent assertion.']
- **limit-small-unnormalized-trace** (hypothesis): Scalar compression requires a projection with strictly positive unnormalized matrix trace below the stated threshold. Its preservation of finite generation and torsion-freeness additionally requires those properties of the original group. Blocked use: ['Replace the sum of diagonal traces by its average.', 'Apply the corner-inverse construction without the strict small-trace hypothesis.', 'Claim torsion-freeness of the free product without torsion-freeness of the original factor.']
- **limit-spectral-separation-membership** (hypothesis): Reduced-algebra membership of the upper projection uses a separated spectral subset of an operator in the represented reduced algebra. A von Neumann kernel dimension alone does not supply this membership. Blocked use: ['Infer reduced-algebra membership from a nonintegral kernel dimension without proving regular spectral separation.', 'Replace the separated-band argument by a numerical observation of a kernel.']
- **limit-common-parameter-order** (hypothesis): The geometric constants, transverse parameters, accuracies and finite-test constants are fixed before increasing a common final tower parameter. Uniform estimates cannot be replaced by separate choices for different layers. Blocked use: ['Choose a different growing parameter for each layer.', 'Allow earlier fixed constants to depend on the growing parameter or on a layer index.', 'Treat qualitative eventual inequalities as verified at an arbitrary finite parameter.']
- **limit-table-selection-existence** (nonconstructive): Successful coefficient and permutation tables are selected through concentration, avoidance probabilities and successive choices over the cutoffs. These arguments do not provide a practical global construction bound. Blocked use: ['Advertise the table-existence argument as an efficient optimizer.', 'Assume arbitrary independent table choices satisfy every expansion requirement.']
- **limit-expansion-before-pruning** (formal_scope): The uniform centered incidence gap is proved for the auxiliary tower before pruning. The survivor is controlled through deletion losses and recovery properties rather than an assertion of expansion on every surviving centered subspace. Blocked use: ['Transfer the auxiliary centered spectral gap unchanged to the pruned graph.', 'Apply the constant-space deletion bound to an arbitrary vector subspace.']
- **limit-specific-prefix-recovery** (hypothesis): Prefix recovery requires nonbacktracking paths in the prescribed gated tower with the same oriented word and sufficient radius on the appropriate side. It does not identify the layer by itself. Blocked use: ['Use foldedness alone as a prefix-recovery theorem for an arbitrary labeled graph.', 'Recover stream coordinates beyond the radius allowed by the statement.', 'Infer equality of layer indices without the additional height-separation argument.']
- **limit-actual-cover-girth** (hypothesis): The geometric construction requires the girth condition on the actual voltage cover, including cyclically nonbacktracking closed walks with repeated traversals. A girth check on simple base circuits is insufficient. Blocked use: ['Apply component embedding and torsion conclusions after checking only the base graph.', 'Ignore closed walks with repeated vertices or repeated edges in the holonomy tests.']
- **limit-formal-edge-convention** (formal_scope): The Cayley graph retains formal generator edges even when endpoints coincide. Degree, embedding and forest assertions use this convention, and loops and parallel-edge circuits must remain visible. Blocked use: ['Simplify the formal graph by identifying edges with coincident endpoints before applying the forest conclusion.', 'Discard parallel-edge circuits when checking the residual adjacency hypothesis.']
- **limit-algebraic-cycle-directness** (formal_scope): Cycle directness concerns algebraic direct sums and finite-support chains over a field. It does not establish uniqueness or convergence for infinite sums of copy cycles. Blocked use: ['Extend the cycle-coordinate isomorphism to arbitrary infinite sums without a topology and a convergence proof.']
- **limit-copy-stabilizers** (formal_scope): Copy stabilizers embed into the torsion-free deck group but need not be trivial. The incidence count remains valid for disconnected covers and must use deck-aligned indexing. Blocked use: ['Assume the action on the set of copies is free.', 'Count incidences by treating different deck representatives as distinct indexed copies.', 'Assume every source component is the entire voltage cover.']
- **limit-word-detector-regime** (hypothesis): The word detector assumes a nonempty cyclically nonbacktracking closed walk, the specified length-to-girth bound and a matrix size exceeding the resulting word-length bound. It establishes nonidentity of a word as a matrix identity, not success of every evaluation. Blocked use: ['Apply the detector to arbitrary long words at the fixed matrix size.', 'Conclude that every sampled voltage assignment detects every nontrivial walk.', 'Infer a cryptographic hardness guarantee from polynomial nonidentity.']
- **limit-infinite-twisted-projections** (nonconstructive): The discrete twisted projections use infinite norm-convergent coefficient series and continuous functional calculus in a small nonzero twist regime. Their definition is not an exact finite-support formula for a projection. Blocked use: ['Treat a truncated coefficient sum as an exact idempotent.', 'Include the zero twist in the stated nonzero-projection conclusion.', 'Certify nonvanishing from an operator-norm defect alone while omitting the coefficient-size comparison.']
- **limit-magnetic-factor-multiplicity** (formal_scope): The finite magnetic factor shifts the effective twist and multiplies projection identity trace by the full matrix multiplicity. It is an auxiliary representation rather than a substitute for the full regular representation. Blocked use: ['Discard the matrix multiplicity in the endpoint trace estimate.', 'Replace regular-norm approximation by agreement in the finite magnetic representation.']
- **limit-finite-box-norm-certification** (approximation): A finite coordinate compression gives an upper regular-norm estimate only with the specified nilpotent coordinates, bounded group supports, coefficient bounds and boundary correction. Compression alone supplies only the lower estimate. Blocked use: ['Use the spectrum or norm of an arbitrary finite truncation as an exact regular spectral certificate.', 'Omit the boundary correction or the absolute coefficient row and column bounds.']
- **limit-simultaneous-edge-assignment** (nonconstructive): The same edge assignment must satisfy both the cover-girth tests and the full regular-norm approximation across the tower. The final assignment is selected by simultaneous existence and countable compactness. Blocked use: ['Combine independently successful girth and approximation assignments as though they were the same assignment.', 'Present a finite random sample as certification of all countably many requirements.']
- **limit-fourier-mask-leakage** (approximation): Finite Fourier masks approximate the smooth masks uniformly but need not vanish exactly outside their active supports. Vanishing of the cover spectral projection outside the window follows from its small operator norm there. Blocked use: ['Assume exact compact support for the truncated Fourier masks.', 'Replace the inactive-leakage estimate by an unsupported exact-zero assertion.']
- **limit-bounded-rank-moment-count** (hypothesis): The closed-walk counting estimate requires bounded support cycle rank and excludes walks with singleton undirected edges. Parallel edges are distinct throughout the count. Blocked use: ['Apply the count to unrestricted closed walks using only a degree bound.', 'Erase cycle-closing edges from the core encoding.', 'Use centering to discard a term whose repeated undirected edge appears in opposite orientations.']
- **limit-compression-and-perturbation-thresholds** (hypothesis): Compression requires a small deletion norm from the reducing upper space. Contour stability requires a perturbation smaller than the contour radius, and equivalence of upper projections uses the stronger perturbation condition. Blocked use: ['Apply upper-space compression when its deletion bound is unproved.', 'Infer projection equivalence from a perturbation satisfying only the weaker resolvent condition.', 'Use the contour estimate without the complementary spectral ceiling.']
- **limit-weighted-overlap-loss** (hypothesis): Trimming and total restriction loss require the weighted identity size, including multiplicities of lifted base vertices and the ball-size cost from Cauchy–Schwarz. An unweighted local diagonal estimate does not replace this control. Blocked use: ['Discard repeated lifts of a base vertex inside a trimming ball.', 'Drop the ball-size factor when summing removed restrictions over copies.', 'Add a further layer-cardinality or matrix-dimension factor to a trace already summed over those indices.']
- **limit-residual-forest-norm** (hypothesis): The residual adjacency estimate requires an actual countable forest with bounded degree and bounded matrix edge weights. It does not apply to a residual graph whose cycles have not been excluded. Blocked use: ['Use the square-root-degree bound for an arbitrary graph.', 'Infer small forest error from small edge weights without also controlling the alphabet-dependent degree.']
- **limit-equivariant-trace-comparison** (formal_scope): Disjoint assigned domains give the spectral comparison, but their order need not be equivariant. Canonical trace comparison instead uses the bounded equivariant restriction operator over all indexed copies. Blocked use: ['Compare canonical traces using only the ordinary assigned-domain projection equivalence.', 'Assume equivariance of the domain assignment from its disjointness.', 'Replace the all-copy incidence space by a chosen subset of copies without a new trace argument.']
- **limit-no-useful-construction-size** (unknown): A useful bound on the size of the final construction is ABSENT. The argument selects a sufficiently large finite parameter after fixed choices and takes no final limit group or limit projection. Blocked use: ['Publish a concrete construction-size or execution-cost guarantee from the existence theorem.', 'Describe the final counterexample as obtained by taking a limit of the constructed groups or projections.']

Set $$U=(\\lambda_K(t_1),\\ldots,\\lambda_K(t_N)),\\qquad\n A=pU^*Up.$$ Then $A$ is invertible in the unital corner $pM_N(C_r^*(K))p$, and $$e=(Up)A^{-1}(Up)^*\n \\label{scalar:projection-formula}$$ is a projection in the scalar algebra $C_r^*(K)$ with $\\tau_K(e)=a$.         }

The operator $JE$ has closed range. The enclosed spectral projection $F_A$ satisfies $$\\|F_A-F\\|\\leq\\frac{u}{r_0-u}.\n \\label{spec:projection-distance}$$ Its enclosed spectrum lies in $[1-u,1+u]$; all the other spectrum of $A$ is at most $1-b+u$. <a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We construct a finitely generated torsion-free discrete group whose reduced group <i>C</i><sup>∗</sup>-algebra contains a projection other than zero and the identity. This disproves the Kadison–Kaplansky projection conjecture.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

The projection form of the Kadison–Kaplansky conjecture asserts that the reduced group $C^*$-algebra of a torsion-free discrete group contains no projections other than zero and the identity. For a discrete group $H$, this algebra is the operator-norm closure of its left regular group algebra on $\ell^2(H)$, and its canonical trace is $\tau_H(x)=\langle x\delta_1,\delta_1\rangle$. The conjecture concerns scalar projections in this completion. We prove the following result.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *There exist a finitely generated torsion-free discrete group $G_{\mathrm{proj}}$ and a projection $e\in C_r^*(G_{\mathrm{proj}})$ such that $$0<\tau_{G_{\mathrm{proj}}}(e)<\frac12.$$ In particular, $e$ is neither zero nor the identity.*

</div>

Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> resolves the projection form of the Kadison–Kaplansky conjecture negatively. The group is given by an infinite graphical presentation followed by a free product with a finitely generated free group. Every parameter is finite, although no useful bound on its size is sought. The choices of labeling tables and edge voltages are specified by finite distributions and proved simultaneous existence statements.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Context

Kadison traces his free-group projection problem to Kaplansky’s question about idempotents in simple unital $C^*$-algebras (Kadison 2008, 222–24) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Kadison2008](#ref-Kadison2008)]. Pimsner and Voiculescu proved the free-group case by computing the $K$-theory of reduced crossed products by free groups (Pimsner and Voiculescu 1982, 131) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[PimsnerVoiculescu1982](#ref-PimsnerVoiculescu1982)]. A central route to projectionlessness is integrality of the canonical trace on $K_0(C_r^*(H))$, where the trace on matrices is the *unnormalized* sum of the diagonal traces. For torsion-free groups, the trace is integral on the image of reduced assembly with trivial coefficients (Lück 2002, Theorem 0.3) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Luck2002](#ref-Luck2002)] <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[BGW2016](#ref-BGW2016)], (Baum, Guentner, and Willett 2016, Proposition 10.3). Surjectivity therefore gives scalar projectionlessness. The survey (Gómez Aparicio, Julg, and Valette 2019, sec. 4.5.3) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[GJV2019](#ref-GJV2019)] explains the distinct scalar and stable formulations.

This implication covers substantial classes of groups. Higson and Kasparov proved Baum–Connes with coefficients for groups acting properly by affine isometries on Hilbert space, including countable amenable groups (Higson and Kasparov 2001, Theorem 9.1 and Corollary 9.2) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[HK2001](#ref-HK2001)]. Mineyev and Yu established the coefficient-free hyperbolic case and deduced the projection conjecture for torsion-free subgroups of hyperbolic groups (Mineyev and Yu 2002, Theorems 20–21) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[MY2002](#ref-MY2002)]; Lafforgue later established the hyperbolic case with arbitrary coefficients (Lafforgue 2012, Theorem 0.4) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Lafforgue2012](#ref-Lafforgue2012)]. The group constructed here has a different geometry: its presentation encodes an infinite tower through embedded nilpotent covers.

For the group $G_{\mathrm{proj}}$ in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, the complex group ring $\mathbb C[G_{\mathrm{proj}}]$ has no scalar idempotents other than zero and one (OpenAI 2026, Corollary 1.2(ii)) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[OpenAIBass2026](#ref-OpenAIBass2026)]. Thus the projection $e$ lies in the reduced completion but not in the group ring. The companion also gives integer trace range on algebraic $K_0(\mathbb C[G_{\mathrm{proj}}])$ and on its image in $K_0(C_r^*(G_{\mathrm{proj}}))$ under the comparison map induced by inclusion (OpenAI 2026, Corollary 1.2(i) and Remark 10.1) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[OpenAIBass2026](#ref-OpenAIBass2026)]. This does not assert integrality on the whole completed $K_0$, where $[e]$ has nonintegral trace.

Expanders also underlie the obstructions of Higson, Lafforgue, and Skandalis for groupoids, coarse spaces, and group actions with coefficients (Higson, Lafforgue, and Skandalis 2002) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[HLS2002](#ref-HLS2002)]. Their group-action statements concern assembly with coefficients, whereas the trace obstruction here concerns trivial coefficients.

The later analysis of expander index obstructions by Willett and Yu (Willett and Yu 2012) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[WY2012](#ref-WY2012)] gives another relevant spectral and coarse-geometric precedent. Its group consequences likewise use coefficients.

Austin constructed rational group-ring operators with irrational von Neumann kernel dimension (Austin 2013) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Austin2013](#ref-Austin2013)]. Such a dimension alone does not imply that the kernel projection lies in the reduced $C^*$-algebra. Here a *uniform separated spectral band* for a finite matrix group-algebra operator in its regular representation gives reduced-algebra membership by continuous functional calculus. Scalar compression is applied after this matrix projection has been obtained.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The obstruction and the proof strategy

The first task is to obtain a matrix projection whose unnormalized trace is strictly between zero and $1/2$. A spectral projection in the group von Neumann algebra is insufficient. We construct a finite self-adjoint matrix $T$ over the group algebra of a torsion-free group $\Gamma_{\mathrm{proj}}$ whose regular spectrum has a nonempty band near $+1$, separated from all remaining spectrum. Continuous functional calculus then places its upper projection $p_0$ in $M_N(C_r^*(\Gamma_{\mathrm{proj}}))$. The difficult part is to retain that gap while making the unnormalized trace small.

Begin with finite sets $A_i,B_i$, indexed by $i\in\mathbb Z$, and bipartite incidences between $A_i$ and $B_k$ for $|i-k|\le1$. Their normalized operators preserve constant vectors and contract the orthogonal complements by a common positive amount. Two versions of the same tower serve different purposes: $Y^0$ provides expansion and $Y^\#$ provides recovery of long paths from words in a finite alphabet. They agree after deleting a uniformly small fraction of vertices. The survivor $Y$ is the base of a voltage cover $P$: its vertices are pairs $(v,f)$, with $f$ in a torsion-free nilpotent group, and an edge changes the second coordinate by its assigned group element. The voltages give large girth and will also realize the required operators.

The analytic model is indexed by a central Fourier variable $t$. Twisted-convolution projections with effective parameters $t$ and $t-\delta$, for a small positive $\delta$, give a nonzero fiber projection for $0<t<\delta$. Normalized combinations of the constant vectors on at most two adjacent layers produce an eigenvalue $+1$, while expansion separates all the other positive spectrum. As $t$ approaches either endpoint, these supporting layers move toward an end of the tower and their fiber trace decreases. A finite magnetic representation is essential to obtain small trace at both endpoints. The result is a summable trace estimate even after the geometric weights needed for intersections of copies have been included.

One edge voltage and one matrix weight per edge approximate this model in the full regular norm. The same finite sampling construction enforces the girth required for the group presentation. Long-word recovery then embeds components of $P$ as indexed copies in the Cayley graph of $\Gamma_{\mathrm{proj}}$. A common-root order confines every earlier intersection with a copy to one finite tree ball. Removing those intersections leaves disjoint domains; the remaining Cayley edges form a forest. The trace estimate controls the loss in the upper spaces caused by the removed balls, and a forest norm estimate controls the unused edges. Together they preserve the separated band for the finite matrix $T$. For the trace estimate, we restrict a vector to every indexed copy and project to its local upper space. This map is equivariant, although the assignment of domains need not be. Disjointness and the same weighted loss estimate bound its retained and removed parts. Its injectivity on the global upper space then bounds the unnormalized trace by the total trace of the local upper spaces.

The final passage to a scalar projection is a separate free-product argument. Scale and comparison in reduced free products were studied by Dykema and Rørdam (Dykema and Rørdam 1998, 2000a, 2000b) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[DR1998](#ref-DR1998), [DR2000](#ref-DR2000), [DRII2000](#ref-DRII2000)]. Recent work on selfless reduced free products and comparison by trace (Flores et al. 2026; Robert 2025) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[FKOP2026](#ref-FKOP2026), [Robert2025](#ref-Robert2025)] also underlies a conditional argument of Sauers (Sauers 2026) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Sauers2026](#ref-Sauers2026)] that turns a nonintegral matrix projection over $C_r^*(H)$ into a scalar projection over $C_r^*(H*\mathbb Z)$. Such a reduction still requires an actual nonintegral matrix projection. Here Proposition <a href="#scalar:compression" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="scalar:compression">3</a> gives a direct quantitative estimate at trace below $1/2$: adjoining $N$ free generators compresses $p_0$ to a scalar projection of the same trace. This gives $G_{\mathrm{proj}}=\Gamma_{\mathrm{proj}}*\mathbb F_N$, preserving finite generation and torsion-freeness. The same small trace also places $[p_0]$ outside the coefficient-free reduced assembly image for $\Gamma_{\mathrm{proj}}$.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Organization

Section <a href="#sec:scalar" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:scalar">2</a> reduces Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> to the small unnormalized matrix-trace target stated in Section <a href="#sec:setup" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:setup">3</a>. Sections <a href="#tow:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tow:section">4</a>–<a href="#prune:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prune:section">6</a> construct the auxiliary graph $Y^0$ with expanding incidences, the gated graph $Y^\#$ with path recovery, and their common pruned induced subgraph $Y$.

Section <a href="#geo:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geo:section">7</a> proves the embedding, overlap, forest, and torsion conclusions under a girth hypothesis on the voltage cover $P$. Section <a href="#nil:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="nil:section">8</a> constructs the deck group and the small-trace fiber projections. Section <a href="#sam:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sam:section">9</a> identifies the model’s upper band, then chooses voltages and matrix weights satisfying both the girth hypothesis and the regular operator approximation.

Section <a href="#spec:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spec:section">10</a> passes the upper spectral band from the model to the cover and then to the Cayley operator, and bounds its unnormalized trace using all indexed copies. Section <a href="#sec:conclusion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:conclusion">11</a> applies scalar compression to complete the proof.

<a id="sec:scalar"></a>
### From a small matrix projection to a scalar projection

We first isolate a reduction that will be useful independently of the construction. Its trace convention is essential. For related results on the scale and comparison of projections in reduced free products, see Dykema–Rørdam (Dykema and Rørdam 1998, 2000a, 2000b) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[DR1998](#ref-DR1998), [DR2000](#ref-DR2000), [DRII2000](#ref-DRII2000)]. The explicit row estimate used here is proved below. For a discrete group $\Lambda$, let $$(\lambda_\Lambda(g)\xi)(h)=\xi(g^{-1}h),\qquad
 C_r^*(\Lambda)=
 \overline{\lambda_\Lambda(\mathbb C[\Lambda])}^{\,\|\cdot\|}.$$ The canonical trace is $\tau_\Lambda(x)=\langle x\delta_1,\delta_1\rangle$. It is tracial on finite group-algebra sums by taking the identity coefficient, and hence on their norm closure. It is faithful: if $x\ge0$ and $\tau_\Lambda(x)=0$, then $x^{1/2}\delta_1=0$. Since $x^{1/2}$ commutes with the right regular action, it annihilates every basis vector and is zero. On matrices we always use the *unnormalized* extension <a id="scalar:matrix-trace"></a>$$\tau_{\Lambda,N}(x)=\sum_{j=1}^N\tau_\Lambda(x_{jj}).
 \label{scalar:matrix-trace}$$ The same formula applies to positive equivariant operators on finite regular multiples. In particular, $\tau_{\Lambda,N}(p)>0$ for a nonzero projection $p$.

<a id="stmt-scalar-subgroup"></a>
<div id="scalar:subgroup" class="lemma">

**Lemma 2** (Reduced subgroup inclusion). *If $\Lambda$ is a subgroup of a discrete group $K$, the inclusion of group algebras extends to an isometric unital inclusion $C_r^*(\Lambda)\subseteq C_r^*(K)$, preserving the canonical traces and their finite matrix extensions.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-scalar-subgroup"><summary>Proof of stmt-scalar-subgroup</summary>

*Proof.* Choose one representative $v$ from every left $\Lambda$-orbit $\Lambda v$ in $K$. The unitary $\delta_\gamma\mapsto\delta_{\gamma v}$ identifies the restriction of the left regular action to $\ell^2(\Lambda v)$ with the left regular action on $\ell^2(\Lambda)$. Thus the representation restricted to $\mathbb C[\Lambda]$ is a direct sum of regular representations, so its norm is precisely the reduced norm on $\Lambda$. Taking closures gives the inclusion. The identity diagonal proves preservation of trace; summing diagonal entries proves the matrix statement. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-scalar-compression"></a>
<div id="scalar:compression" class="proposition">

<a id="scalar:projection-formula"></a>
**Proposition 3** (Scalar compression after a free product). *Let $\Lambda$ be a discrete group and $p\in M_N(C_r^*(\Lambda))$ a projection with $$0<a:=\tau_{\Lambda,N}(p)<\frac12.$$ Put $K=\Lambda*\mathbb F_N$, where $t_1,\ldots,t_N$ freely generate $\mathbb F_N$, and regard $p$ as a matrix over $C_r^*(K)$ by Lemma <a href="#scalar:subgroup" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="scalar:subgroup">2</a>. Set $$U=(\lambda_K(t_1),\ldots,\lambda_K(t_N)),\qquad
 A=pU^*Up.$$ Then $A$ is invertible in the unital corner $pM_N(C_r^*(K))p$, and $$e=(Up)A^{-1}(Up)^*
 \label{scalar:projection-formula}$$ is a projection in the scalar algebra $C_r^*(K)$ with $\tau_K(e)=a$. If $\Lambda$ is finitely generated and torsion-free, then so is $K$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-scalar-compression"><summary>Proof of stmt-scalar-compression</summary>

*Proof.* To bound $U$ below on $p\ell^2(K)^N$, we separate noncancelling inputs, whose outputs are disjoint, from cancelling inputs, whose size both before and after applying $U$ is controlled by the trace $a$. Use free-product normal forms, writing entries of the free factor in reduced free letters. Let $\mathcal R$ consist of the identity and the normal words whose first factor is in $\mathbb F_N$. Every element of $K$ has a unique expression $\gamma v$ with $\gamma\in\Lambda$ and $v\in\mathcal R$: remove its initial $\Lambda$-factor, if present. Thus $\mathcal R$ is a transversal for the orbits used in Lemma <a href="#scalar:subgroup" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="scalar:subgroup">2</a>.

<a id="scalar:pointwise"></a>
Write $a_j=\tau_\Lambda(p_{jj})$, so $a_j\ge0$ and $\sum_j a_j=a$. If $\xi=p\xi\in\ell^2(K)^N$, put $$m_v=\|\xi|_{\Lambda v}\|^2,\qquad
 \sum_{v\in\mathcal R}m_v=\|\xi\|^2.$$ Restriction to an orbit commutes with $p$. Applying Cauchy–Schwarz to the projection on that orbit gives $$|\xi_j(v)|^2
 =|\langle \xi|_{\Lambda v},p\delta_{(v,j)}\rangle|^2
 \le a_jm_v.
 \label{scalar:pointwise}$$ Here $\|p\delta_{(v,j)}\|^2
=\langle p\delta_{(v,j)},\delta_{(v,j)}\rangle=a_j$.

<a id="scalar:good-output"></a>
In coordinate $j$, call a position bad if its reduced word starts with $t_j^{-1}$, and good otherwise. Split $\xi$ accordingly as $\xi=\xi_{\mathrm g}+\xi_{\mathrm b}$. Every bad position belongs to $\mathcal R$; its first letter determines its bad coordinate uniquely. Therefore <a id="scalar:bad-input"></a>$$\|\xi_{\mathrm b}\|^2
 \le a\sum_{v\in\mathcal R}m_v=a\|\xi\|^2.
 \label{scalar:bad-input}$$ Left multiplication by $t_j$ sends every good position to a word starting with $t_j$. These output sets are disjoint for different $j$, and multiplication is injective within each coordinate. Hence $$\|U\xi_{\mathrm g}\|^2
 =\|\xi_{\mathrm g}\|^2
 \ge(1-a)\|\xi\|^2.
 \label{scalar:good-output}$$

<a id="scalar:bad-output"></a>
Bad outputs may coincide, and require a separate estimate. For an output word $h$, let $J(h)$ be the coordinates whose bad input $v_{h,j}=t_j^{-1}h$ contributes to $h$. By <a href="#scalar:pointwise" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="scalar:pointwise">[scalar:pointwise]</a>, $$|(U\xi_{\mathrm b})(h)|^2
 \le\left(\sum_{j\in J(h)}a_j\right)
      \left(\sum_{j\in J(h)}m_{v_{h,j}}\right)
 \le a\sum_{j\in J(h)}m_{v_{h,j}}.$$ The map $(h,j)\mapsto v_{h,j}$, restricted to these bad inputs, is injective: the first letter of $v_{h,j}$ determines $j$, and then $h=t_jv_{h,j}$. Summing over $h$ gives $$\|U\xi_{\mathrm b}\|^2\le a\|\xi\|^2.
 \label{scalar:bad-output}$$ Equations <a href="#scalar:good-output" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="scalar:good-output">[scalar:good-output]</a> and <a href="#scalar:bad-output" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="scalar:bad-output">[scalar:bad-output]</a>, and the triangle inequality, imply <a id="scalar:lower-bound"></a>$$\|U\xi\|\ge c_a\|\xi\|,\qquad
 c_a=\sqrt{1-a}-\sqrt a>0.
 \label{scalar:lower-bound}$$

Consequently $A\ge c_a^2p$ in its C\*-corner. Also $UU^*=N1$, so $A\le Np$. In particular, its inverse belongs to the corner itself: explicitly, $$A^{-1}=\frac1N\sum_{k=0}^\infty(p-A/N)^k$$ converges in norm because $\|p-A/N\|\le1-c_a^2/N<1$. Continuous functional calculus also supplies $A^{-1/2}$ there. The row $V=UpA^{-1/2}$ belongs to $M_{1,N}(C_r^*(K))$ and satisfies $V^*V=p$. Thus $e=VV^*$ is the scalar projection in <a href="#scalar:projection-formula" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="scalar:projection-formula">[scalar:projection-formula]</a>. Writing $V=(V_1,\ldots,V_N)$ and using traciality, $$\tau_K(e)
 =\sum_j\tau_K(V_jV_j^*)
 =\sum_j\tau_K(V_j^*V_j)
 =\tau_{K,N}(p)=a.$$ It is therefore neither zero nor one.

Finally, adjoining the finite set $t_1,\ldots,t_N$ preserves finite generation. A nontrivial element of a free product is conjugate either to a nontrivial factor element or to a cyclically reduced word with at least two alternating factor entries. Positive powers of the latter have strictly increasing reduced length. If both factors are torsion-free, neither case has finite order. This proves the last assertion. ◻

</details>

It remains to construct a finitely generated torsion-free $\Gamma_{\mathrm{proj}}$ and a projection satisfying the small *unnormalized* trace hypothesis of Proposition <a href="#scalar:compression" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="scalar:compression">3</a>. The next sections do so.

<a id="sec:setup"></a>
### Conventions, parameters, and the matrix target

We will prove the following intermediate statement.

<a id="stmt-setup-matrix-target"></a>
<div id="setup:matrix-target" class="theorem">

**Theorem 4** (Matrix construction). *There exist a finitely generated torsion-free discrete group $\Gamma_{\mathrm{proj}}$, a finite integer $N\ge1$, and a self-adjoint element $T\in M_N(\mathbb C[\Gamma_{\mathrm{proj}}])$, in its regular representation, with a nonempty separated spectral subset whose spectral projection $p_0$ satisfies $$p_0\in M_N(C_r^*(\Gamma_{\mathrm{proj}})),\qquad
 0<\tau_{\Gamma_{\mathrm{proj}},N}(p_0)<\frac12.$$*

</div>

Here and below a spectral subset is *separated* if its distance from the rest of the spectrum is positive. Its indicator is continuous on the spectrum. The spectral projection therefore belongs to the unital $C^*$-algebra containing the operator, by continuous functional calculus. We also use the equivalent contour-resolvent formula. These facts, together with polar decomposition, are standard bounded-operator spectral theory (Reed and Simon 1980, chaps. VI–VII) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[ReedSimon1980](#ref-ReedSimon1980)].

In graph coordinates it is convenient to use right translations $$(\rho_\Lambda(g)\xi)(h)=\xi(hg).$$ The unitary $\mathcal I\xi(h)=\xi(h^{-1})$ satisfies $\mathcal I\rho_\Lambda(g)\mathcal I=\lambda_\Lambda(g)$. This identification fixes the identity vector and leaves finite matrix indices unchanged, so it preserves all traces used here. Thus a finite matrix sum of right translations specifies the same kind of reduced-algebra element as in Theorem <a href="#setup:matrix-target" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="setup:matrix-target">4</a>.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Graphs and labels

Edges have length one and may be traversed in either direction. Whenever a positive orientation is specified, reverse traversal carries the formal inverse label. A labeled graph is *folded* if, at each vertex, a prescribed oriented label occurs on at most one outgoing edge. A path is nonbacktracking when it never immediately reverses an edge; a closed path is cyclically nonbacktracking if this also holds across its chosen basepoint. Closed walks and their repeated traversals are allowed as presentation relators.

For a labeled group presentation we retain the formal Cayley edges: a positive generator and its initial vertex specify an edge even if different formal generators have the same group value. All degree and forest assertions are about this graph. This convention also specifies an unambiguous left action on oriented edges. Intrinsic distances and balls in an embedded copy are measured using its own graph edges.

All vertex Hilbert spaces use counting measure. A biregular incidence from a $B$-side of degree $D'$ to an $A$-side of degree $d$ is normalized by $(dD')^{-1/2}$. It maps the normalized constant vector on one side to the normalized constant vector on the other. When using probabilities to prove a bound for that operator, we pass explicitly to uniform probability on each side.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### The growing parameter

Let $\ell$ be a positive integer, eventually sufficiently large, and set <a id="setup:parameters"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 d&=2^{4\ell},& D&=d^2,& s&=10\ell,& r&=7\ell,\\
 n_0&=1000,&
 H_i&=(2n_0+1)s+1+|i| \quad(i\in\mathbb Z).
\end{aligned}
\label{setup:parameters}$$ Write $H_0=(2n_0+1)s+1$, and put <a id="setup:lengths"></a>$$L_i=\frac{100H_i}{\log_2d},\qquad
 g_i=\frac{H_i}{100\log_2d}.
